# Release digest for week of 2026-11-09 to 2026-11-16

## Week in numbers

- **446 releases**: 245 with new features, 196 with fixes, 4 with breaking changes.
- **653 merged pull requests** and **685 issues closed**.
- From merge to release: median **< 1 minute** (90% within 13 minutes).
- Busiest repository: [DirectPublic](https://github.com/Cratis/DirectPublic) (75 releases).
- Current daily release streak: **104 days**.

## Added

- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.33.0)): `CompilationResult.SpecificationRefusedTypedContextDescriptors` publishes wrapper-ready typed context descriptors for every implementation role of the validated model in `SpecificationRefusedValue`, so hosts that run the healthy remainder of a model with refused specifications get descriptors for rule predicates, reactors and other bodies, not only command handlers. Failed compilations keep their original failure descriptors and diagnostics in `TypedContextDescriptors`. (#1315)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.168.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.168.0)): New `AdditiveEventTypeSchemaEvolutionEventStores` setting lists the event stores where Chronicle accepts an additive change to a registered event type's schema without a new generation. A change is additive when it only adds optional, nullable or defaulted properties. The list is empty by default, so every event store keeps strict validation; set it on every Chronicle server, in `chronicle.json` or with `Cratis__Chronicle__AdditiveEventTypeSchemaEvolutionEventStores__0=<event store>`. (#5946)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.168.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.168.0)): Chronicle records an `EventTypeSchemaEvolved` system event when it accepts an additive schema change. (#5946)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.168.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.168.0)): The event type check result now includes a classification of the schema difference: identical, tolerated, additive or breaking. (#5946)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.167.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.167.0)): `FileSystemCachingTokenProvider` has a new constructor that takes a cache scope, such as the server address and client id. A cached token is only reused for the same scope. The existing constructor works as before; give each server and identity its own cache file when you use it. (#5598)
- **Stage** ([Release v6.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v6.1.0)): Stage verifies embedded reference packs against SHA-256 manifests before using them. (#1073)
- **Stage** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): Applications are drawn from the resolved design system: every ui profile that draws from it carries it, in the live Stage, `cratis render` and the generated application (#1147)
- **Stage** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): Adopted free screens render as Scene screens owned by their adopting slice, with provisions as bindings, and an unprovided need is refused with a reason naming the screen and the shape (#1150)
- **Stage** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): ESM v56 models are admitted
- **AI** ([Release v7.168.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.168.2)): Deploy and compatibility gates must ask the system of record. A gate that predicts Chronicle event-type registration either calls Chronicle's read-only registration check or carries a contract spec against Chronicle's comparison for every change class. The enum rule in the application profile links to it (#1225)
- **AI** ([Release v7.168.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.168.2)): Requests for human-only production actions must include necessity evidence: the actual trigger and an independent check against real system behavior that the action is needed. Reviewers ask whether the action is needed, not just whether the procedure is right (#1225)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.166.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.166.2)): The standalone Workbench can bind to a configured address through `ChronicleWorkbenchOptions.Address`, for example `localhost` to listen only on loopback; leaving it empty keeps listening on all interfaces (#5597)
- **AI** ([Release v7.168.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.168.0)): Screenplay UI composition guidance for free screens: screens declared anywhere, `shape` contracts, `uses screen` with provisions and `from` mappings, aliases, storyline screens, severities and design-only screens (#1181)
- **AI** ([Release v7.168.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.168.0)): Slice design guidance for designing screens before slices exist
- **AI** ([Release v7.168.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.168.0)): Design system guidance for component anatomy as the rendering contract, with `maps to` and variant-to-property links
- **AI** ([Release v7.168.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.168.0)): Scene Design guidance for agents: design operations, the design tool loop, refusing free styling, and proposals into the design-system draft
- **Scene** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): Scene renders a resolved design system: modes, token roles per theme and mode, and component anatomy (parts, variants, states) that components read their look from, with a default design system that keeps today's look (#316)
- **Scene** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): The C# engine applies every Scene edit with the same outcomes as the TypeScript engine, held to a shared fixture corpus (#317)
- **Scene** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): `Cratis.Scene.Screenplay`, one two-way bridge between Screenplay and Scene, including design systems and free screens with their provisions (#318)
- **Scene** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): `Cratis.Scene.Design` and `@cratis/scene.design`: the design catalogue, design operations with checks and refusals, screen sets with storylines, template mode and template sets, and the design session with its Chronicle events (#319)
- **Scene** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): `Cratis.Scene.Design.AI`: the design tool loop on Cratis.AI — screens from prompts and images, edits, variations, tweaks, design-system proposals, and establishing a design system from host sources (#320)
- **Scene** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): `@cratis/scene.editor`: the screen, UI profile, template, form, navigation, string and sample data editors, and a design-system editor (#321)
- **Scene** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): `@cratis/scene.design.react`: the design canvas with artboards along storylines, chat, variations, tweaks, revisions and element-anchored comments (#322)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.30.0)): Rendering copies the model's assets folder into the rendered application, so `/assets/<path>` (for example a layout's logo) works in the published app as in the sandbox (#1041)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.32.0)): A `screen` can be declared at the top level, in a module or in a feature, as well as in a slice (#1319)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.32.0)): `shape` declares the fields a screen needs; `data <Shape>[]` without `via query` states a need, optionally named with `as`
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.32.0)): `uses screen <Name>` lets a slice adopt a screen and provide each need with `data <Need> via query <Query> [by …]`, mapping differing field names with `<field> from <path>`
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.32.0)): The compiler checks provisions against the screen's needs and the read model (PLAY1149–PLAY1164); an unprovided need is a warning that fails a `--warnaserror` release check, and an unadopted screen is reported as design-only
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.32.0)): Sample data `shape`s can name a declared `shape`, and storylines, `navigate to` and `show` reach free screens
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.32.0)): ESM v56 carries free screens and slice adoptions, and the UI contract carries adopted screens with their adopting slice
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.32.0)): MCP typed proposals for shapes, free screens, adoptions and provisions
- **AI** ([Release v7.167.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.167.0)): `cratis-engineering-inherited-work` skill in the `cratis/engineering/core` profile: reconstructs the original goal from the issue, pull request and commits, checks for newer architecture, competing pull requests and work already on main, and recommends continue, rework or obsolete with evidence. Takeover verifies the source head against the handoff, re-checks claimed results, reconciles earlier review findings and preserves contributor credit through merge commits, never rebase, amend or force-push. Closing someone else's pull request stays behind the existing authorization rules. Adapted from the MIT-licensed tstack `modernize` and `takeover` skills. (#1014)
- **AI** ([Release v7.166.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.166.0)): The `cratis-ux-review` skill critiques a rendered screen against its user goal: it gathers browser captures across viewports, themes and states, applies Nielsen's heuristics plus Cratis passes for hierarchy, consistency with Cratis Components, loading, empty and error states, accessibility risk and copy, and reports severity-rated findings with evidence, the smallest fix using existing components and an observable acceptance outcome (#1015)
- **AI** ([Release v7.166.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.166.0)): The React and Components profiles include the skill, and `cratis-arc-react-page` and `cratis-components-accessibility` link to it
- **Components** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): `DataPage.MenuItem` `command` receives the current selection as a `DataPageSelection<T>` snapshot: the selected item in single selection mode, or a readonly `selectedItems` collection in multiple selection mode. Existing commands without arguments keep working. (#489)
- **Components** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): `DataPage.MenuItem` `disabled` also accepts a predicate over the current selection, combined with `disableOnUnselected`; a disabled item never runs its command. (#489)
- **Components** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): `DataPageSelection<T>` is exported for typing menu actions; declare the menu item as `MenuItem<T>` to get typed rows. (#489)
- **AI** ([Release v7.165.5](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.165.5)): A conformance fixture checks that a frontend task with no browser operator available reports the UI check as unverified and stops at sign-in instead of driving a browser itself (#977)
- **AI** ([Release v7.165.5](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.165.5)): A verification spec fails when the frontend developer agent, in its canonical form or any adapter, is granted a browser tool (#977)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.12.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.12.1)): Chronicle 19.163.0: A certificate authority in the kernel for hosted federation. It issues, renews and revokes peer certificates, and keeps its keys wrapped with an operator-supplied key-encryption key that is read from a file. (cratis/chronicle#5901)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.12.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.12.1)): Chronicle 19.163.0: One-time enrollment codes, and `RedeemFederationEnrollment` so an application's kernel obtains its certificate and starts its link without a restart. The .NET client exposes it as `IChronicleClient.Federation`. (cratis/chronicle#5901)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.12.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.12.1)): Chronicle 19.163.0: The `chronicle.federation.enroll` capability, bundled into the administrator role, and a configuration allow-list of application client ids that may redeem a code without being administrators. (cratis/chronicle#5901)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.12.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.12.1)): Chronicle 19.163.0: Per-subject federation policy: a peer is admitted by CA chain, issued-certificate registry and policy, and each direction can be suspended, resumed or revoked on its own. (cratis/chronicle#5902)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.12.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.12.1)): Chronicle 19.163.0: A dialer-initiated session that carries both directions over one connection through a new `Serve` call, and a dial-only mode that starts no listener and opens no port. (cratis/chronicle#5903)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.12.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.12.1)): Chronicle 19.164.0: `IChronicleClient.FederationPolicies` to set a subject's federation policy (target event store and namespace, allowed event types, direction), suspend, resume and revoke a subject or one direction, list policies, and read link status per subject: whether a session is open, last activity, events and bytes per direction, throttling and the last error. (cratis/chronicle#5916)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.12.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.12.1)): Chronicle 19.164.0: The enrollment status of a kernel now reports whether its dialer session to the authority is connected, the time of the last successful exchange and the last error, so an application can prove the link works before it considers itself connected. (part of cratis/chronicle#5916)
- **cli** ([Release v3.138.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.138.0)): `cratis run` accepts `--stage-setting KEY=VALUE`, repeatable, to pass settings to the Stage container. The last value for a key wins, and explicit settings override those the run command supplies itself. Not for secrets. (#567)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.12.0)): A published Native AOT acceptance sample backed by Chronicle, with specs for protected read-model lookups by key and for the calling user (#3634)
- **cli** ([Release v3.137.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.137.0)): `--level screenplay`, `code`, `code-ai` and `ai` run the packaged Cratis builder at the four modes Studio offers: Screenplay only, static code, static code refined by AI and AI only. `--target axon` renders for Axon Framework instead of Cratis.
- **cli** ([Release v3.137.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.137.0)): At `code-ai` the model is rendered deterministically and an agent then implements the behavior the model describes on top of the generated code. At `ai` the agent implements the slices from the model without rendering. Every agent prompt ends with *Instructions from the model*: the slices' descriptions from the order's `context.hints`, the specifications to meet and the feedback people left.
- **cli** ([Release v3.137.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.137.0)): `--docker` runs the builder in a packaged image with the project mounted, and `--image` chooses the image. A builder in a container reads its connection from `CRATIS_STUDIO_URL`, `CRATIS_STUDIO_ORGANIZATION`, `CRATIS_BUILDER_ID` and `CRATIS_BUILDER_TOKEN`.
- **cli** ([Release v3.137.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.137.0)): `--ai studio` sends an agent's model calls through Studio's AI gateway, on the organization's providers, over a loopback bridge that speaks the OpenAI and Anthropic APIs. Generators get `STUDIO_AI_URL` and `STUDIO_AI_TOKEN`.
- **cli** ([Release v3.137.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.137.0)): A Dockerfile and a manual workflow for the packaged builder image.
- **cli** ([Release v3.137.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.137.0)): Documentation for running the Cratis and Axon builders locally at every level.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.165.0)): Preview on the Workbench Projections page works for `projection ... => graph` declarations. It shows the nodes and edges the events build and, through a view, the nested rows a graph view query returns.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.165.0)): A "New graph" button loads an Organization example (people, groups, MEMBER_OF, PART_OF), and the help panel explains graph declarations.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.165.0)): A `PreviewGraphProjection` command compiles the declaration, runs up to 1000 events of the event store through the real graph changeset builder against an in-memory graph, and evaluates graph views over the result, with compile errors, view errors and per-event failures returned in the result.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.165.0)): The kernel evaluates graph views in memory, held to the same shared query vectors as the client and Arc.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.165.0)): Documentation for previewing graph projections in the Workbench.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.16.0)): Granted users over the `Http` transport. The collector serves the list of users a desk has granted to an application's publication through a bearer-authenticated `access` endpoint with `ETag` support, and the application caches it and polls on `NotesOptions.AccessRefreshInterval`.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.16.0)): `NotesOptions.AccessMaxStaleness`. The application refuses everyone when it has never fetched the list, or when the last confirmation is older than this window, and a revocation takes effect at the next poll.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.16.0)): `NoteCollectingOptions.AccessStores`, so a collector can follow the desks that decide who may give feedback.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.16.0)): `NotesOptions.ApplicationKind` (`Published` or `Staged`) and a `NoteGivenOnStagedApplication` public event. A collector sees `ReceivedNote.IsStaged`, and a collector that does not know the event keeps working.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.16.0)): Documentation for granted users over `Http`, staged sessions and the access endpoint.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.15.0)): Object storage abstraction with S3, Azure Blob and volume providers, and a check that key backups never share a bucket with data (part of #18)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.15.0)): Discovery of a Production's Chronicle event stores, namespaces and read-model databases, reporting unclaimed databases (part of #19)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.15.0)): Scheduled database backups with manifests, tiered retention and a maximum age, owned by the Understudy (part of #20)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.15.0)): CMS-encrypted key backups read from the Vault (part of #21)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.15.0)): `Cratis.Showrunner.Understudy.Contracts` with backup facts, Kubernetes events and Prometheus metrics that carry no tenant or person labels (part of #29)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.15.0)): The `cratis/showrunner-understudy-image` job image and its publishing, and documentation for Understudies
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.14.0)): Production: `vault.mode: Managed` runs HashiCorp Vault for Chronicle's keys, in a development tier (one node, file storage) or a production tier (three nodes on Raft, with a Chronicle token that reaches only its own key paths). Vault is unsealed by a Secret (the default), by a cloud key service (AWS KMS, Azure Key Vault or Google Cloud KMS) or by a transit Vault. Chronicle is configured for Vault compliance storage automatically (part of #17)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.14.0)): Production: `vault.mode: External` takes the address of your Vault and a token Secret (part of #17)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.14.0)): Production: `federation` opens Chronicle's federation listener on port 35100, makes it the hosted authority, or makes it dial out and serve its public events to an authority, which is the sending side of a one-way link (part of #15)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.14.0)): Production: `database.backup` runs the Percona Backup for MongoDB agent next to every member of a managed database (part of #16)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.14.0)): Performance: `application.strategy`, `imagePullSecrets`, `probes` (readiness, liveness and startup), `resources`, `nodeSelector`, `volumes` and `volumeMounts`, `initContainers` and `egress` shape the pod of an application you built
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.14.0)): Production status reports `vault` and the `VaultModeUnchanged`, `VaultReady` and `FederationConfigured` conditions
- **AI** ([Release v7.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.165.0)): `Cratis.AI.ReverseProxy` NuGet package: a YARP proxy that serves `/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/messages` and `/v1/models` and routes each request to the endpoint that serves the requested model, with streaming responses passed through as they arrive.
- **AI** ([Release v7.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.165.0)): A pluggable `IModelTransport`, with a direct HTTP transport for backends on the same network and a seam for carrying requests to another machine, such as through a tunnel.
- **AI** ([Release v7.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.165.0)): `ModelDiscovery`, which detects the protocols and models of an Ollama, LM Studio, vLLM or llama.cpp backend.
- **AI** ([Release v7.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.165.0)): `IModelAccess`, which lets nobody in until the host application allows callers explicitly; the caller's own credential is never forwarded to a backend.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.29.0)): A `Stage:Sandbox:Notes:Enabled` setting that hosts the Notes backend and the model-references endpoint in sandbox mode. It is off by default, so sandbox behavior does not change unless a host turns it on.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.29.0)): An optional `Stage:Sandbox:Notes:ModelReferencesFile` so notes from a sandbox session carry the ids of the model elements they are about.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.29.0)): A "Feedback in a play session" reference page describing the settings and how to relay notes with a per-session collector token.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.13.0)): `FeedbackAccessGranted` and `FeedbackAccessRevoked` public contract events, which a desk such as Direct appends to its outbox to decide who may give feedback in an application.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.13.0)): `NotesOptions.AccessMode` (`Everyone` or `GrantedUsers`) and `NotesOptions.AccessStores`, also bound from configuration. `Everyone` is the default and keeps today's behavior.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.13.0)): `IFeedbackAccess` so a host can ask on the server whether the signed-in user may give feedback.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.13.0)): Module and feature levels in the feedback dialog for notes about a part of the application.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.13.0)): A "Who can give feedback" documentation page and a description of what a note can be about.
- **Arc** ([Release v23.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.11.0)): Proxy generator `--check` option compares committed TypeScript clients with what the compiled backend would generate, without writing or deleting files. It lists missing, changed and obsolete files and exits `2` on drift, `0` when up to date and `1` on errors. (#3637)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.11.0)): Proxy generator `--validation-report=text|json` and `--validation-report-file=<path>` options explain which inspected server validation rules are projected into generated TypeScript clients and why others stay server-side. (#3638)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.11.0)): `QueryOptions.MaxQueuedEmissionsPerSubscription` (default 256) caps how many emissions can queue for one observable-query subscription. When a subscriber falls further behind than that over WebSocket, Server-Sent Events or the demultiplexer hub, Arc ends the subscription and the client reconnects with fresh data. (#3633)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.11.0)): Command results now report `IsMissingHandler` (`isMissingHandler` in TypeScript) when a command has no registered handler, so clients can tell a missing handler apart from other failures. `CommandForm` validation treats such a result as not run, with the reason `missingHandler`, instead of as a failed validation. (#3869)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.31.0)): Specifications can assert the exact, ordered tags of an appended event: write `tag <name>` lines under a `then <Event>` step, or `no tags` to assert that the event carries none. Duplicates and order count; omitting both leaves tags unchecked. Models that use tag expectations select ESM version 55, and tag expectations in `given`, `when`, redelivery and typed examples are refused with PLAY1148. The TypeScript compiler, Monaco editor, VS Code grammar, MCP server and documentation all support the new form. (#1138)
- **AI** ([Release v7.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.164.0)): Kubernetes worker jobs can carry a priority class: set `Cratis:AI:ContainerRuntime:PriorityClassName` to the name of a Kubernetes `PriorityClass` so the scheduler can rank agent workers against other workloads such as CI runners; when it is not set, worker jobs are unchanged. The cluster owner creates the priority class itself (#1158)
- **AI** ([Release v7.162.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.162.0)): Add a shared OpenTelemetry observability rule, scoped to telemetry files (part of #469)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.30.0)): The VS Code extension offers the PLAY0469 identifier-payload repair. It is never part of fix-all, and Apply needs explicit consent after a full preview that lists the semantic addresses the repair retires (part of #341)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.30.0)): The Screenplay MCP server now advertises PLAY0469, PLAY0516 and PLAY0664 in `repair-capabilities`, with optional per-repair flags for fix-all eligibility, individual review, event-contract changes and retired semantic addresses (part of #341)
- **AI** ([Release v7.161.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.161.0)): A language model completion can take one or more images (`IVisionLanguageModel`, `LanguageModelImage`); PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP are accepted, and usage is metered under the purpose like any other completion (#1148)
- **AI** ([Release v7.161.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.161.0)): Anthropic, Z.AI, OpenAI, Azure OpenAI and OpenAI-compatible providers send images with the prompt through the new `IAIProviderImageClient` (#1148)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.10.0)): `CommandForm` now reports validation metadata (pending, freshness, verdict and validated version, separately for client and server validation) through the form context, the form handle and `onStateChange`, so a form can tell a current verdict from one kept for earlier values or a validation that is still pending. A missing command handler is reported as an `error` verdict for now; see https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/issues/3869 for distinguishing it (#3624)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.9.0)): `[ProtocolOnly]` for model-bound commands that run through the command pipeline in-process but are not exposed over HTTP (execute or validate routes), the command introspection catalog or generated TypeScript proxies. It is a routing control, not an authorization control: validation, authorization and filters still run. (#3774)
- **AI** ([Release v7.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.160.0)): The Pi subagent resolves an agent's `modelTier` and reviewer independence to concrete models from a user-owned `cratis-models.json`, and falls back visibly to the session model when only one provider is configured. (#866)
- **AI** ([Release v7.159.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.159.1)): The issue design-note skill has an evaluation for an ambiguous canonical comment, so the skill must ask which comment is canonical instead of guessing (#976)
- **AI** ([Release v7.159.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.159.1)): The Screenplay UI composition skill explains how to repair action-subject advisories one occurrence at a time (part of #944)
- **AI** ([Release v7.159.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.159.0)): New `@cratis/pi-direct` Pi package for Cratis Direct local sessions: when a Direct runner launches Pi for a work item, it claims the work, keeps the claim alive with heartbeats and releases it with an outcome; in any other Pi session it does nothing. Install with `pi install npm:@cratis/pi-direct`; setup is in the [Pi harness guide](https://www.cratis.io/direct/runner/harnesses#pi). (#1161)
- **AI** ([Release v7.159.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.159.0)): A session refuses to start when its launch proof or launch environment is missing or invalid, and Direct write tools stay blocked for the rest of a session once its claim is lost.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.28.0)): The sandbox serves Screenplay graph queries that `collect` nested rows: each query is read as a Chronicle graph view and executed through the tenant-aware Arc graph session, so every root comes back as the read model with its related rows nested. (#1144)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.28.0)): ESM 54 is admitted and the collect members are classified, so a model that uses `collect` is no longer refused at admission.
- **Components** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): `DatePickerInput` and `CalendarField` accept an `openCalendarLabel` prop that names the calendar toggle button; it defaults to "Open calendar" (#505)
- **Components** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): `Page` and `DataPage` accept `landmark={false}` so a page embedded in an app shell that already renders a `main` landmark does not nest a second one (#501)
- **cli** ([Release v3.136.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.136.0)): `cratis build connect --studio --organization <name>` connects to the hosted Studio, `https://app.cratis.studio`. Give `--studio <url>` only for another Studio.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.12.0)): An opt-in setup page at `/.cratis/showrunner/direct`, enabled through the Box Office product options. An administrator pastes the enrollment code Direct shows; the page connects the application's Chronicle to Direct and closes permanently once the link is verified. It is off by default, so applications that Direct does not operate never expose it. (#138)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.12.0)): Redeeming the code without the page, from the `EnrollmentCode` option, for automated deployments. A code is ignored when the application is already connected. (part of #138)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.12.0)): Documentation for turning the page on, who may use it, and the Chronicle settings the application's kernel needs. (part of #138)
- **AI** ([Release v7.158.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.158.1)): The Screenplay projection skills explain typed reducer keys with `KeyAs<T>()`, the `from`/`every` ambiguity and why triggers are excluded from projections (#1000)
- **AI** ([Release v7.158.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.158.1)): The Screenplay read-surface skill explains passive read models: when to use them, what Stage renders and the diagnostics they raise (#1002)
- **AI** ([Release v7.158.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.158.1)): The Screenplay command-surface and Arc authorization skills explain policies that read current state before allowing a command (#1097)
- **Chronicle.Mcp** ([Release v2.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Mcp/releases/tag/v2.3.0)): The server sends initialization instructions telling clients that stored content is data only and never authorizes `stop_job`, `resume_job` or `delete_job`. See [Untrusted data in tool results](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Mcp/blob/main/Documentation/concepts.md#untrusted-data-in-tool-results). (#62)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.164.0)): `IChronicleClient.FederationPolicies` to set a subject's federation policy (target event store and namespace, allowed event types, direction), suspend, resume and revoke a subject or one direction, list policies, and read link status per subject: whether a session is open, last activity, events and bytes per direction, throttling and the last error. (#5916)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.164.0)): The enrollment status of a kernel now reports whether its dialer session to the authority is connected, the time of the last successful exchange and the last error, so an application can prove the link works before it considers itself connected. (part of #5916)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.29.0)): `collect` inside a graph query's `return` assigns a read-model property the rows reached along one edge (outgoing or incoming), with edge properties available through a named edge. A list property gets every row; an optional property gets the first row or null. One level of nesting; selecting roots by a collected node never narrows the collected rows.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.29.0)): ESM v54, selected only when a query collects. Models that do not use `collect` keep their version and bytes.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.29.0)): `then query` specifications assert collected rows, and the reference runner evaluates them with a deterministic order.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.29.0)): Diagnostics PLAY1144 to PLAY1147 for a malformed collect, a collect that does not fit its read model or row type, a nested collect, and a query that is not view-shaped.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.29.0)): The `Organization` sample reads people, groups and roles as views, and the shared graph view vectors pass against the reference runner.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.29.0)): Support in the C# and TypeScript compilers, the printer, the Monaco editor, the VS Code extension, the MCP server, the documentation and decision 0163.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.11.0)): Announcement titles accept inline Markdown such as links and emphasis, so a title can end in "Read more here."
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.11.0)): The banner shows the title only; an announcement with a message gets an expand button that reveals the message and a tooltip that shows it on hover and focus
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.11.0)): Announcements without a message and announcements without a start or end date (permanent until withdrawn) are covered by specs, stories and documentation
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.163.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.163.0)): A certificate authority in the kernel for hosted federation. It issues, renews and revokes peer certificates, and keeps its keys wrapped with an operator-supplied key-encryption key that is read from a file. (#5901)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.163.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.163.0)): One-time enrollment codes, and `RedeemFederationEnrollment` so an application's kernel obtains its certificate and starts its link without a restart. The .NET client exposes it as `IChronicleClient.Federation`. (#5901)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.163.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.163.0)): The `chronicle.federation.enroll` capability, bundled into the administrator role, and a configuration allow-list of application client ids that may redeem a code without being administrators. (#5901)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.163.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.163.0)): Per-subject federation policy: a peer is admitted by CA chain, issued-certificate registry and policy, and each direction can be suspended, resumed or revoked on its own. (#5902)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.163.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.163.0)): A dialer-initiated session that carries both directions over one connection through a new `Serve` call, and a dial-only mode that starts no listener and opens no port. (#5903)
- **AI** ([Release v7.158.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.158.0)): Providers can be restricted to a specific list of team members with the `SetAIProviderAllowedTeamMembers` command, so a shared credential is only used for work started by those members (#998)
- **AI** ([Release v7.158.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.158.0)): Provider listings and configured providers expose `AllowedTeamMembers` and `HasAllowedTeamMembersSelection`, so an unrestricted provider can be told apart from one that denies everyone (#998)
- **AI** ([Release v7.158.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.158.0)): `IWorkInitiation` carries the team member who started a piece of work; agent runs, conversations, pool dispatch and worker launches check it against a restricted provider's allowed team members and refuse the work when the member is unknown or not on the list (#998)
- **AI** ([Release v7.158.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.158.0)): `IProviderSelectionAuthority` decides who may change a provider's allowed team members; the default implementation denies every change until the host replaces it (#998)
- **AI** ([Release v7.158.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.158.0)): New documentation page on restricting providers to team members (#998)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.8.0)): `Cratis.Arc.Neo4j.Chronicle` runs Chronicle graph view queries against Neo4j: register with `WithNeo4jGraphViews()` or `AddCratisNeo4jGraphViews()`, inject `IGraph` and call `Nodes<TView>()` to get typed, nested records. Tenancy follows the existing graph session, and values are always sent as query parameters.
- **Arc** ([Release v23.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.8.0)): `Cratis.Arc.Neo4j.Chronicle.Testing` adds `UseGraph()` and `UseGraphSubstitute()` to replace `IGraph` in specifications.
- **Arc** ([Release v23.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.8.0)): Documentation and a sample that read the graph through a view.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.162.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.162.0)): Graph views: `[ViewOf<TNode>]`, `[Follows<TEdge>]` and `[FromEdge]` declare a read model anchored at one node that follows declared edges to nested views and exposes node and edge properties. Views are validated and produce the canonical view definition JSON every Chronicle client shares.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.162.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.162.0)): `IGraph.Nodes<TView>()` queries a view with member-selector clauses and hydrates typed records. Host packages execute reads by implementing `IGraphQueryExecutor`.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.162.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.162.0)): `GraphScenario` queries views against its in-memory graph, with `SeedNode` and `SeedEdge` to seed it.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.162.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.162.0)): Analyzers CHR0078 to CHR0081 report a view anchored at a non-node, `Follows` on a non-edge, a relation target that is not a view, and a view that is also a node or edge.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.162.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.162.0)): Documentation for reading a graph with views.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v7.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v7.11.0)): `@ViewOf`, `@Follows` and `@FromEdge` declare a graph view: a read model anchored at one node that follows declared edges to nested views and exposes node and edge properties. They work from Kotlin data classes and from Java records.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v7.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v7.11.0)): `GraphViews.definitionsFor` builds the canonical view definitions, and `GraphViewDefinition.toJson` writes the JSON every Chronicle client shares.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v7.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v7.11.0)): An invalid view fails with an `InvalidGraphView` carrying a named reason, such as `EdgeNotIncidentOnRoot` or `RecursiveView`. Every graph still registers before the failure is thrown.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v7.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v7.11.0)): Documentation for declaring graph views.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.161.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.161.0)): The MongoDB storage provider probes the connected server's capabilities at startup, and the hosting documentation describes Azure DocumentDB compatibility and its measured gaps (#5642)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.16.0)): `graphs.NewView` declares a graph view: a read model anchored at one node that follows declared edges to nested views and exposes node and edge properties.
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.16.0)): `(*Graph).WithViews` attaches views to a graph. `Declaration.ViewDefinition` and `Definition.Views` expose the canonical view definition, as `Canonical` or `CanonicalWithSchema`, the JSON every Chronicle client shares.
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.16.0)): An invalid view fails `Build()` with a typed reason that works with `errors.Is`, such as `graphs.EdgeNotIncidentOnRoot` or `graphs.RecursiveView`.
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.16.0)): Documentation for declaring graph views.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.64.0)): `@viewOf`, `@viewKey`, `@viewProperty`, `@follows` and `@fromEdge` declare a graph view: a read model anchored at one node that follows declared edges to nested views and exposes node and edge properties.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.64.0)): `GraphViewDefinitionBuilder` and `GraphViewDefinitionSerializer` build and serialize the canonical view definition, the JSON every Chronicle client shares. `Graphs.viewDefinitions` exposes the definitions of the discovered views.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.64.0)): An invalid view is reported through the graph failures with a named reason, such as `EdgeNotIncidentOnRoot` or `RecursiveView`, and does not stop other graphs from registering.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.64.0)): Documentation for declaring graph views.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.24.0)): `Chronicle.Graphs.View` declares a graph view: a read model anchored at one node that follows declared edges to nested views and exposes node and edge properties.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.24.0)): `Chronicle.Graphs.ViewDefinition` builds the canonical graph view definition and encodes it as the JSON every Chronicle client shares.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.24.0)): The `view` macro registers views inside a graph module. A view that cannot work fails compilation with a named reason, such as `EdgeNotIncidentOnRoot` or `RecursiveView`.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.24.0)): Documentation for declaring graph views.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.160.0)): Event store subscriptions can be restricted to listed source namespaces; observers of unlisted namespaces are revoked and the subscriber drops their events (#5874)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.160.0)): Azure Key Vault encryption keys migrate from earlier secret names to hashed names with an operator-listed scope check, verified copies and guarded removal (#5747)
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.65.0)): Bearer routes produce signed v2 forwarded-identity envelopes for verified OAuth grants. The envelope carries the credential kind, credential id and cache epoch from active token introspection, and binds to the forwarded access token through a SHA-256 hash (#344) (part of #294)
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.65.0)): The forwarded-identity readiness report marks bearer OAuth-grant flows as ready (#344)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.28.0)): The compiler warns when a projection or reducer copies a secret concept into a read model, where it would be included in query results. (#1213)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.28.0)): The opt-in `personas` completeness check reports a screen whose bound commands and queries are all definitely denied to every declared persona's synthesized caller (`PLAY1143`), so a screen nobody can use is no longer silent. Unknown or undecidable gates and screens without bindings are never reported. Available in the C# and TypeScript compilers and as `--completeness PLAY1143`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.28.0)): Hosts can opt into an executable model that excludes broken specifications while preserving their errors and stable identities. (#1310)
- **cli** ([Release v3.135.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.135.0)): `cratis run --detach` waits until the Stage is ready, prints its addresses and container name, and exits with the sandbox left running; if startup fails or is cancelled, the container is removed again (#566)
- **cli** ([Release v3.135.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.135.0)): `cratis run --timeout <seconds>` sets how long the command waits for the Stage to become ready before stopping it; detached runs default to 300 seconds, foreground runs wait until you stop them (#566)
- **cli** ([Release v3.135.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.135.0)): `cratis stop` stops a sandbox started by `cratis run`, by name or with `--all`, and reports Not found when the named sandbox is not running (#566)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.10.0)): `Cratis.Showrunner.Cue`: `AddCue` with `CueOptions.Tours` (the tour names the application accepts), the `StartTour` command that records the first start of a tour once per person, the `StartedTour.ByMe` query, `IViewerTours` for reading the history on the server, and `ISignedInViewer` to replace how a person is told apart (the `sub` claim by default). The history is kept under a hashed event source with the person as compliance subject, so an erasure request finds it.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.10.0)): `@cratis/showrunner.cue`: the generated proxies for that command and query.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.10.0)): `@cratis/showrunner.cue.react`: the tour player. `TourOverlayShell` plays a script of sections, chapters and steps against one clock, with chapter list, subtitles, transport, pointer, callouts and an optional written chapter for each step; `useCueTours` and `useTourNudge` connect a tour button to the backend so it points at a tour until the person has taken it.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.10.0)): A configurable voice-over. `CueProvider` takes a `speech` configuration: `createHttpSpeech` connects any text-to-speech service reached over HTTP (the address, availability address, request body, headers and cache size are the application's), and an application with its own pipeline provides a `CueSpeech` of its own. Without one the tours play silently with subtitles.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.10.0)): Every text of the tour's chrome is a label the application can replace, and the application can guard a tour's stage with its own error boundary. The styles use the `--cratis-*` tokens, with `--cue-viewport-top` and `--cue-z-index` to fit an application shell.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.10.0)): Documentation for Cue: how it works, adding it to an application, writing a tour, configuring the voice-over, remembering who has taken a tour, and the event reference.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.159.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.159.0)): The Workbench has a Constraints page where authorized operators can start a constraint reindex, behind a confirmation that governed appends may wait, with a link to the job. (#5888)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.7.0)): Availability now passes the calling system's client id to the audience source. For a system caller, `AudienceSubject.SystemClientId` is read from the `client_id` claim, or `azp` when `client_id` is absent, so an `IAudienceSource` can return the system-wide grants plus that client's own. Callers without a client id pass none, and person callers are unchanged. (#3851)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.9.0)): GuestList availability answers a system caller from the client id on its audience subject: it gets the grants every system holds plus the grants for that client id, and a caller with no client id or an unknown one gets only the product-wide ones. Person and tenant behavior is unchanged. Requires Cratis.Choreography 2.9.0.
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.9.0)): `AudienceSubject.SystemClientId` carries the calling system's client id so an `IAudienceSource` can return the system-wide grants plus that system's own. It is empty when the caller names none, and existing callers and constructors are unchanged. (#226)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.8.0)): `AudienceLevel.NamedSystem` and the `SystemClientId` and `SystemKind` (`None`, `Mcp`, `Api`, `Other`) types, so a decision can name one system without changing what the `System` level means (#135)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.8.0)): `GrantAudience` and `WithdrawAudience` take an optional `ClientId` and `Kind`; a named-system decision must name both and no tenant or user, and a withdrawal removes that system's grant only
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.8.0)): `SystemsDeclared` and `SystemDeclaration`: a product reports the systems that call it, through `IKnownSystems`, and the operator reads them as the `ProductSystems` query, so an operator can list systems with their client id and kind
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.8.0)): `IGuestList.ForNamedSystem` and `ForSystemCaller`, and `GuestListAudienceSource.GetGrants(subject, clientId)`: a request made by a named system gets the audiences of every system plus its own, never another system's and never a person's
- **Arc** ([Release v23.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.6.0)): `Tenancy.RequireDuplicateHeaderPreservation` refuses startup when header-based tenancy runs on a host that cannot preserve duplicate headers. (#3791)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.6.0)): Untyped `CommandScenario<object>` can turn on decision reads with the new `EnableDecisionReads()` instead of the obsolete `UseDecisionReads()` (#3669)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.6.0)): Read-protection configuration is accepted under `Cratis:Arc:ReadProtection`, with root-level keys still working. (part of #3778)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.6.0)): Configuration key reference and minimal setup guides for Arc alone and Arc with Chronicle. (part of #3778)
- **AI** ([Release v7.157.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.157.0)): Worker runs receive an explicit repository-to-mirror mapping in `CRATIS_WORKER_REPOSITORY_MIRRORS`, and the cache contract names each repository's clone URL and mirror path (`WorkerCacheRepository`, `WorkerCacheAccess`) (#1152)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.158.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.158.0)): An administrator query that lists stored event type schemas carrying annotations (`deprecated`, `contentEncoding`, `examples`, `$comment`), and the `NormalizeEventTypeSchemas` administrator command that removes those annotations from the stored schemas. (#5878)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.157.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.157.0)): `CheckEventTypeRegistrations` kernel command: checks a set of event types against what the kernel has registered, without registering anything, and reports a verdict per event type with the first differing schema keyword and path. (part of #5877)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.157.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.157.0)): `ReindexConstraints` operator command (administrators only) to rebuild the constraint indexes of an event store and namespace on demand. (part of #5880)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.27.0)): The generated availability registration builds an `AvailabilityManifest` and registers `IAvailabilityManifest` as a singleton: the declared audiences, every availability block, a gate entry for each module, feature, slice, command and read model with the slices at or beneath it, and the provided context values with their scope. Every list is sorted, so the same model always renders the same bytes. A model without availability emits no manifest.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.7.0)): `AvailabilityDeclared` and `AudienceDecisionReceived` public events in `Cratis.Showrunner.GuestList.Contracts`, generation 1 and additive, with `EnvironmentName`, `ModelVersion`, `AudienceDecision` and the declaration types they carry.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.7.0)): `AvailabilityReporter` in `Cratis.Showrunner.GuestList.Availability`: reports the Choreography availability manifest once per change, survives restarts, retries in the background and never holds the host back. `GuestListOptions` gains `Environment`, `Version`, `OperatorAudience` and `RetryInterval`.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.7.0)): A receipt for every decision a product records: `AudienceDecisionReceived` is published once per recorded decision, not on re-delivery and not for a decision the product ignored.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.7.0)): `ProductAvailability` (what a product declares per environment and what each report changed) and `AudienceDelivery` (the newest decision each environment received) read models in `Cratis.Showrunner.GuestList.Authoring`.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.7.0)): The documentation page "Report what the model declares" and the new events in the GuestList contracts reference.
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.8.0)): `AvailabilityManifest` and `IAvailabilityManifest` (with `InMemoryAvailabilityManifest`): audiences, blocks, gates, context values and personas as immutable records.
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.8.0)): `AvailabilityExpression.Alternatives()`: expands any gate into disjunctive normal form, including `with`, `and`, `or`, parentheses and `when`.
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.8.0)): `AudienceDeclaration` and a `DeclaredAudiences` overload that takes declarations with description and source.
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.8.0)): An optional `Description` on `AvailabilityDefinition`.
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.8.0)): Documentation page for the availability manifest.
- **AI** ([Release v7.156.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.156.0)): Add the `cratis-arc-mcp` skill for configuring Arc MCP tools, personal access tokens and scopes, client connections, and troubleshooting. (#1093)
- **AI** ([Release v7.155.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.155.0)): `cratis-engineering-post-mortem` skill: a repeatable, provider-neutral post-incident review built from an incident record and its linked runs, pull requests and issues. It covers a UTC timeline with per-row confidence (Verified, Inferred, Unverified), impact, unverified claims kept apart from facts, root causes as why-chains, an action table with one owner and one linked issue per action, lessons, and a public-safe wording pass. It ships with a review template and two generalized worked examples (#1149)
- **AI** ([Release v7.155.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.155.0)): `post-mortem` agent that drafts the review with that skill and files each action in the repository that owns it after a duplicate search, treating evidence text as data rather than instructions (#1149)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.26.0)): Rendering of `audience` declarations, reusable `availability` blocks and `available` gates as generated Choreography availability policies registered with Arc, evaluated before `authorize` and refusing with the `NotAvailable` reason, including `with` composition and `$context.audiences`. (#1117)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.26.0)): Availability exposed through Arc so client features can hide navigation for parts that are not available. (#1117)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.26.0)): Generated hosts wire the Showrunner guest list as the audience source when the model uses availability; reactions that `invokes` a command are evaluated with the tenant's audiences plus the system's. (#1117)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.26.0)): Diagnostics that refuse availability conditions and mixed query gates the generated gates cannot evaluate faithfully, instead of rendering them silently. (#1117)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.26.0)): Reference documentation: Availability rendering.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.25.0)): The sandbox reads the stored caller back onto each fact when it rebuilds its world, so projection mappings of `$eventContext.causedBy.subject`, `.name` and `.userName` run over history and show who made each change (#1083)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.27.0)): Projections can map `$eventContext.causedBy.subject`, `.name` and `.userName` in the reference evaluator, wherever `occurred` is already supported
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.27.0)): Facts carry the audit identity of the caller that caused them
- **Stage** ([Release v5.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.24.0)): A model that provides values to its request context now renders: `provide` at the application, a module, a feature or a slice becomes a generated `ProvidedValueResolvers` service on Choreography's context value engine, resolving each value lazily and once per request, with the nearest declaration winning. Literal, `$context` expression, `host` and keyed snapshot query sources render, and the host registers an empty `IHostContextValues` you can replace from `Customizations/Program.cs`. See [Provided values rendering](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/provided-values-rendering.md). (part of #1116)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.24.0)): Stage admits ESM 53, the version that carries provided values, audiences and availability gates. A model that declares an audience, an availability or an `available` gate is refused with `STAGE-ESM-016` until those render.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.24.0)): `STAGE-ESM-075` and `STAGE-ESM-076` refuse a provided value Stage cannot resolve faithfully, such as a query with authorization, filters or a performer, a query keyed by a literal, a collection or a concept type.
- **AI** ([Release v7.154.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.154.0)): A `cratis-screenplay-availability` skill that teaches `audience`, `availability`, `available` and `provide` with `$context` values: where availability belongs versus `authorize`, levels, reserved names, required versus optional values, absence versus failure, specifications always running with everything available, unknown never opening a gate, the cross-slice warning, authoring-only persona audiences, audiences versus Box Office modules, Chronicle routing audiences and token `aud`, runtime ownership, and review checks for gates no persona can satisfy, unreferenced audiences, unresolvable `provide` keys and rules reading identity-derived values (#1144)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.5.0)): Screenplay generation states `[AvailableFor]` and `[AvailableWith]` gates on commands and read models as `audience`, `availability` and `available for` / `available with` lines. (#3834)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.5.0)): Screenplay generation states values resolved from a read model query, declared in a `ContextValueCatalog`, as `provide ... from query ... by $context...` lines at the application, module, feature or slice the scope names. (#3834)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.5.0)): Diagnostic `SP0080` reports a gate or value that cannot be stated faithfully: a gate registered with `Gate` or `GateNamespace`, a value the host, an expression or code provides, an audience name that is not an identifier, and a scope that names nothing of the model.
- **Arc** ([Release v23.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.5.0)): Diagnostic `SP0081` reports an availability declared without rules, because its definition lives in the host's `AvailabilityCatalog`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.26.0)): `audience` and reusable `availability` declarations with `for`, `when` and `with` lines, combined with `and`, `or` and parentheses, and `available for`, `available when` and `available with` gates on modules, features, slices, commands, queries and screens. Gates combine down the path and are decided before `authorize`; an unknown audience, availability or value never opens a gate (#1301)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.26.0)): Persona `audience` lines, for authoring only, and the `$context.audiences` value.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.26.0)): `provide <name> <Type> from <source>` for scope-driven context values readable in `available when`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.26.0)): ESM v53, written only when a model uses availability or provided values.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.26.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY1130` to `PLAY1141` for malformed, duplicate, unknown and circular audiences and availabilities, unprovided values, and commands that can be unavailable to their invoker.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.26.0)): The same support in the TypeScript compiler, Monaco (hover, completion, go to definition, snippets), the VS Code grammar, rename and the MCP server. The event model board draws a gated slice for the personas that pass its gates and marks it.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.26.0)): A documentation page on audiences and availability, and gates in the Invoicing and Commerce samples.
- **Arc** ([Release v23.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.4.0)): `QueryContext.Values`, `AuthorizationPolicyContext.Values` and `context.Values()` on the FluentValidation context read provided values by name, such as `context.Values.Vessel`. `ProvidedValues` gives the asynchronous and typed reads, and `ValuesAs<T>()` reads them as a typed payload. A command reads them through `CommandContext.ProvidedValues` and `ValuesAs<T>()`, because `CommandContext.Values` already holds the values of the command pipeline. (#3833)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.4.0)): `IContextValueSource`, registered by `AddArcAvailability` on the Choreography engine and on the request's availability context, so gates, handlers, performers, policies and rules in one request share one value and a second request resolves again. (#3833)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.4.0)): An absent value reads as unknown (`null`), a required value that is absent throws `RequiredContextValueAbsentException`, and a failure to resolve throws `ContextValueUnavailableException`, which command and query results report as a retryable error, never a denial. (#3833)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.4.0)): Documentation: https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/context-values.md (#3833)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.6.0)): `Cratis.Showrunner.BoxOffice.Product.Availability`: `AddBoxOfficeEntitlementsAvailability()` supplies the host-provided context value `entitlements` (`provide entitlements Entitlements from host`), readable in `available when` conditions as the tenant's `active` state, `readOnlyReason`, `seats` and each module's `level` (`None`, `Preview`, `Trial`, `Full`) and `usable` (#127)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.6.0)): Documentation on using entitlements in availability conditions and how Box Office modules relate to GuestList audiences
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.25.0)): `provide <name> <Type> [optional] from query <Query> [by <expression>, ...]`, `from host`, `from <literal or $context expression>`, or a `file` reference or inline code block beneath it, at the application, module, feature and slice levels. The nearest declaration wins and an override keeps the type (#1300)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.25.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY1119` to `PLAY1129` for malformed, duplicate, reserved-name, mistyped, unknown-query, observable-query and cyclic provides
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.25.0)): A `Values` property and `ValuesAs<T>()` on `CommandContext`, `QueryContext`, `RuleContext`, `PolicyContext` and the new `ProvideContext`, so code reads provided values by name
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.25.0)): `provide` support in both compilers, the printer, Monaco (completion, hover, validation), the VS Code grammar and the MCP authoring index, with documentation and a multi-file fixture
- **Arc** ([Release v23.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.3.0)): `Cratis.Arc.Availability` package: `AddArcAvailability(...)`, `[AvailableFor]` and `[AvailableWith]` attributes, and `options.Gate<TPart>(...)` / `options.GateNamespace(...)` gates built on Choreography `AvailabilityExpression`. Gates apply down the path (namespace, part, attributes) and the audiences and context values of a request are resolved once. (#3831)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.3.0)): Commands and queries of a part that is not available are refused before `authorize` with the `NotAvailable` reason (`AvailabilityReason.NotAvailable`) and HTTP 403. Unknown denies. (#3831)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.3.0)): `CommandResult` and `QueryResult` carry `AuthorizationFailureReason` and `IsRetryable`; a failure to decide whether a part is available is a retryable error (HTTP 503), never a denial. (#3831)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.3.0)): The `/.cratis/availability` observable query reports what is not available to the current context, with the slice of each part, and re-emits when audiences change. (#3832)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.3.0)): `@cratis/arc/availability` and `@cratis/arc.react/availability`: `AvailabilityProvider`, `RequireAvailable`, `useAvailableItems` and `useAvailability` hide unavailable parts, navigation items and screens and show them again live; command results expose `isNotAvailable`, `onNotAvailable` and `isRetryable`, and `isNotAvailable(result)` reads query results. (#3832)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.3.0)): MCP tools whose command or query is not available are omitted from the tool listing; a call is refused with the `NotAvailable` reason, and the tool outcome now includes `reason` and `isRetryable`. (#3832)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.3.0)): Documentation for [availability](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/availability.md) and [React availability](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/frontend/react/availability.md). (#3832)
- **AI** ([Release v7.153.10](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.153.10)): Spec guidance on choosing the cheapest real proof: hosts, kernels, containers and browsers only prove contracts the repository owns, consumer routing/e2e and service-boundary regression tests stay in the pull-request lane, and retiring a consumer assertion needs a named upstream spec. Run `cratis ai update` to get it. (#1139)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.5.0)): `Cratis.Showrunner.GuestList.Availability`: `AddGuestListAvailability` registers a Choreography `IAudienceSource` over GuestList's local read models. The effective audiences are the union of the system's, the tenant's and the user's grants, each tagged with its level. Nothing is asked of the operator, so the last known state stays in force while the link is down. Grants for audiences the application does not declare are carried, never match a gate and are reported (a warning once per grant; the `UndeclaredGrant` query lists them). Cratis/Choreography#221
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.5.0)): Open sessions update: `IAudienceSource.OnChanged` is raised when a grant or withdrawal changes the product's read models, including for tenants added later.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.5.0)): The `cratis.showrunner.guestlist.audience_lag` metric reports the seconds since the product last received a change to its audiences.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.5.0)): Documentation: Feed the availability engine.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.4.0)): `Cratis.Showrunner.GuestList.Authoring`, the operator side of audiences, which works without Direct: the `GrantAudience` and `WithdrawAudience` commands for a tenant, a user or the system, the `ProductGuestList` and `UndeclaredAudienceGrant` queries, and an outbox reactor that publishes `AudienceGranted` and `AudienceWithdrawn` once, in the order they were decided (#126)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.4.0)): Commands refuse a missing product, an invalid audience name, a tenant or user a level does not need or does not name, and an unknown tenant (through the optional `IKnownTenants`); granting a held audience or withdrawing one that is not held records nothing
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.4.0)): `AddGuestListAuthoring` with `AddressToProducts` and `DeclaredAudiences` options, and `GuestListAuthoring.Policy` for the host's authorization
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.4.0)): Integration specifications that run an operator store and a product store against a real Chronicle kernel
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.4.0)): A guide to granting audiences as an operator, and GuestList in the audience routing table
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.3.0)): `Cratis.Showrunner.GuestList`, the product side of audiences: it receives `AudienceGranted` and `AudienceWithdrawn` through the inbox and records them in the tenant's own namespace (user grants in the tenant's namespace, system grants in the product's), so the newest decision wins, a duplicate is recorded once and a withdrawal removes only its own level's grant (#125)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.3.0)): `IGuestList` and the `GrantedAudience` and `UndeclaredGrant` queries for a tenant's, a user's and the system's audiences and for grants that match no declared audience; they answer from what the product recorded, so the last known state stays when the operator is unreachable
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.3.0)): `AddGuestList` with `Product`, `OperatorStores`, `SubscribeForProduct`, `DeclaredAudiences` and `PollingInterval` options
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.3.0)): A guide to receiving audiences in a product
- **Stage** ([Release v5.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.23.0)): A signal a screen handles with `on <signal>` is bound to Scene's signal trigger when the name is a signal the component's contract declares; a name the contract does not declare stays an application trigger. When the component raises the signal, the runtime runs the behaviors attached to that component through the Scene interaction engine, and `signal.<value>` arguments read the values the component raised. See [Handling a raised signal](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/components.md#handling-a-raised-signal). (part of Cratis/Screenplay#1248)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.2.0)): `Cratis.Showrunner.GuestList.Contracts`: the public events `AudienceGranted` and `AudienceWithdrawn`, which grant and withdraw an audience at Tenant, User or System level, with `Product`, `Level`, `TenantId`, `UserId` and `Audience` fields (#124)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.2.0)): GuestList documentation: overview, how it works and a contract page with the event table
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.7.0)): Availability engine that evaluates `for`, `when` and `with` gates combined with `and`, `or` and parentheses in authored order, with three outcomes (available, unavailable, unknown), where unknown and absence deny and a failure is an exception, never a denial (#220)
