- **Choreography** ([Release v2.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.7.0)): Audience abstraction (`IAudienceSource`) with effective audiences as the union of tenant, user and system grants, grants for undeclared audiences carried and reported but never matching, and a change subscription for reactive invalidation, plus in-memory implementations (#220)
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.7.0)): Request-scoped context values declared at application, module, feature and slice level, resolved lazily once per request with nearest-level override, cycle detection, required and optional values, and providers for read models, literals, expressions, the host and code (#222)
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.7.0)): Environment switch that grants every declared audience, off by default and refused outside local and test environments (#223)
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.7.0)): Documentation for the availability engine
- **Stage** ([Release v5.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.22.0)): The ability catalog, the component placements, the setting declarations and the per-screen backend usage are built from the compiled model and the loaded packages' contracts. A system no loaded package realizes has no abilities, and a component the contract does not declare is not placed. See https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/end-to-end.md (part of Cratis/Screenplay#1248)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.22.0)): A screen that places `component <System>.<Component>` renders as a `Stage:extensionComponent` element carrying the locked package's frontend address, the abilities the contract lets the component invoke, the signals it raises and its placeholder.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.22.0)): The page tells the host which screen was reached (`POST /stage/extensions/activation/screens/<screen>`), so the backends of that screen's systems start before the component's first invocation.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.22.0)): `Samples/Extensions/Notifier`, a minimal extension with a contract, a React component, a placeholder and a C# backend, and specifications that run it from a lock through a real Orleans silo.
- **Scene** ([Release v5.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v5.4.0)): `SignalInteractionTrigger` (`signalName`) in `@cratis/scene.model` and `Cratis.Scene.Model.Interactions`, a new interaction trigger kind next to the built-in, event, interval and application-trigger kinds. Existing triggers and documents are unchanged.
- **Scene** ([Release v5.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v5.4.0)): `resolveSignalBehaviors(attachments, signalName)` in `@cratis/scene.engine`, which collects the bindings for a raised signal in the same deterministic order as the other triggers and never matches an application trigger of the same name.
- **Scene** ([Release v5.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v5.4.0)): Documentation of the signal trigger in the extension contract guide and the glossary.
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.64.0)): Opt-in `ForwardSessionContext` service setting that adds the browser session start time (`urn:cratis:session:started`) and sign-in provider (`urn:cratis:session:sign-in-provider`) claims to the forwarded principal (#327)
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.63.0)): `RequireVerifiedEmail` on OIDC providers: the callback is refused unless the signed-in person has exactly one e-mail address marked verified, and the person is answered like a failed sign-in without a session. `email_verified` is mapped from the user info endpoint when the ID token does not carry it. (#329)
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.63.0)): `DenialResponse: "FailedSignIn"` on OIDC providers: when identity details deny a session from that provider, AuthProxy ends the session and answers like a failed sign-in instead of showing the 403 page. (#329)
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.63.0)): `AuthorizationParameters` on OIDC providers, for parity with OAuth providers; parameters the authentication handler owns are rejected at startup. (#329)
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.63.0)): Documentation for gating an e-mail-code provider at sign-in. (#329)
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.1.0)): Payments: `Cratis.Showrunner.Payments` is a provider-neutral contract for taking payments. `IPaymentProvider` covers customers, first and one-off payments, charges against a customer's permission, subscriptions and refunds, and `IPaymentWebhookHandler` is what an application registers to hear that a payment changed.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.1.0)): `Cratis.Showrunner.Payments.Mollie` implements the contract against Mollie. `AddMolliePayments` registers it and `MapMolliePaymentWebhook` maps the webhook, which reads the payment back from Mollie before the application hears of it, so a forged callback cannot change what a payment says.
- **Showrunner** ([Release v2.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Showrunner/releases/tag/v2.1.0)): Documentation for Payments, with the options and the webhook's answers.
- **AI** ([Release v7.153.8](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.153.8)): Opt-in security hardening for Docker workers, and per-job resource limits applied to the worker container. (part of #1047)
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.62.0)): Each `OidcProviders[]` and `OAuthProviders[]` entry can set its own browser-session lifetime with an optional `Session` block (`Lifetime`, `SlidingExpiration`). A provider value can only shorten the global `Session:Lifetime` or turn sliding expiration off; a non-positive value or one longer than the global lifetime stops startup with a message explaining the fix. Providers without the block keep the global settings, sessions that already exist keep their current lifetime, and personal access tokens and other non-browser credentials are not affected. Stored user tokens follow the same lifetime, so they never outlive the session (#326)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.21.0)): React and mount extension components, with `user`, `tenant`, `locale`, `theme`, public `settings`, `strings`, `invoke`, `raise` and `on`, and the `useUser`, `useTenant`, `useLocale`, `useTheme`, `useSettings`, `useStrings`, `useInvoke`, `useRaise` and `useTrigger` hooks. Language and theme changes update the context. (#1070)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.21.0)): A placeholder, marked busy, while a component loads, and a readable message when the module does not export it or exports it as the other kind. A package's placeholder is shown in a frame that cannot run script.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.21.0)): `invoke` goes through the host with one interaction identity across waits and refuses an ability outside the contract in the browser as well as on the host. Secrets never reach the browser: the page asks only for the identity, the public settings and invocations.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.21.0)): `@cratis/scene.extensions` is a shared module on the page's import map, so an extension's hooks read the context Stage provides.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.21.0)): Documentation of the browser side in [Run extension components](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/components.md).
- **Arc.Elixir** ([Release v1.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Elixir/releases/tag/v1.1.0)): A `:arc_analyzer` compiler. Turn it on with `compilers: [:arc_analyzer] ++ Mix.compilers()` in `mix.exs`, or run `mix igniter.install cratis_arc`. The installer is safe to run twice and updates every app of an umbrella.
- **Arc.Elixir** ([Release v1.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Elixir/releases/tag/v1.1.0)): Rules `ARCE0001` (a module declares a schema and `handle/2` but does not `use Arc.Command`), `ARCE0002` (a command or query is both anonymous and restricted), `ARCE0003` (an `@observe` query has neither `:watch` nor `:topics`), `ARCE0004` (a command appends to Chronicle itself) and `ARCE0005` (two commands or queries resolve to the same route).
- **Arc.Elixir** ([Release v1.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Elixir/releases/tag/v1.1.0)): Configuration with `config :cratis_arc, :analyzer`: a global `enabled` switch and a severity per rule (`:error`, `:warning`, `:info` or `:off`). `@arc_analyzer ignore: ["ARCE0001"]` turns a rule off for one module, and `mix compile --warnings-as-errors` fails on warnings from the analyzer.
- **Arc.Elixir** ([Release v1.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Elixir/releases/tag/v1.1.0)): A Code analysis section in the documentation with a page for every rule, and a changelog.
- **AI** ([Release v7.153.7](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.153.7)): A `cratis-screenplay-extension-packages` skill for creating, verifying, locking, restoring, vendoring and trusting extension packages: the contract and manifest, settings, components, `screenplay.lock`, `cratis extension new`, `verify`, `restore`, `update` and `vendor`, the publisher allow-list and exit codes. (part of #1121)
- **AI** ([Release v7.153.7](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.153.7)): Guidance on invoking an ability from reactions, workflows and state machines with `$result` and `$failure`, on realizing a system with an extension, and on what is not yet admitted. (part of #1121)
- **AI** ([Release v7.153.7](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.153.7)): Extension components on screens and `given`/`then invokes` ability answers in specifications, as references. (part of #1121)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.20.0)): Browser invocations: a screen component can ask Stage to run an ability, limited to the contract's `invokes` allowlist and the screen's authorization. The request is recorded as a new `ScreenInteraction` origin, and a repeated interaction identity is a retry, not a second run. (part of #1070)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.20.0)): Public settings for extension components: only settings declared public reach the browser; secret settings never do. (part of #1070)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.20.0)): Static placeholders for pages without a browser runtime (previews, exports, first paint). (part of #1070)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.20.0)): Documentation: "Run extension components" in the extensions section. (part of #1070)
- **Templates** ([Release v1.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.9.0)): `Cratis.Templates.Extensions`, a template package of its own, with two templates: `cratis-extension` for a full extension, and `cratis-extension-package` for a frontend-only component package. See the [extension templates guide](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/index.mdx). (#104)
- **Templates** ([Release v1.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.9.0)): `cratis-extension` takes `Backend` (`csharp`, `kotlin`, `go`, `typescript` or `none`) and `Frontend` (`react`, `mount`, `screenplay` or `none`), and every combination is valid, including `none` with `none`. `cratis-extension-package` takes `Component` (`react` or `mount`). Both take `DisplayName`, `Description` and `Publisher`, and the package name is the template name.
- **Templates** ([Release v1.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.9.0)): Every package contains `contract.play` with its manifest (display name, description, icon, marketplace image and requirements), a placeholder icon and image, a static HTML placeholder for each React or mount component, a sample ability or component with specifications, build, test, package, verify and publish scripts, a `.extensionignore` and a README. Each package passes `cratis extension verify` without edits.
- **Templates** ([Release v1.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.9.0)): Backends use the Stage extension SDKs and keep the sample ability in plain code, so a specification calls it without a host. React and mount components run the Scene conformance kit in their specifications.
- **Templates** ([Release v1.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.9.0)): Until the Kotlin, Go and TypeScript backend SDKs and `@cratis/scene.extensions` are published, the generated scripts use a local build when `CRATIS_STAGE_SDK` or `CRATIS_SCENE_SDK` is set, and the README says how.
- **Templates** ([Release v1.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.9.0)): Documentation for the templates, linked with the Screenplay, Stage, Scene and Cratis CLI documentation.
- **cli** ([Release v3.134.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.134.0)): `cratis config --requires <model>` lists everything a model needs from the selected environment - identity provider keys, named secrets (`secrets.<Name>`) and the settings of its extensions (`systems.<System>.<setting>`) - with whether each is set. It never prints a value, and exits with `5` while a key is missing or an extension is not restored. (#577)
- **cli** ([Release v3.134.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.134.0)): `cratis run` admits a model's extensions before starting Docker: each must be pinned in `screenplay.lock` and its publisher and reference allowed in `.cratis/extensions.json`, otherwise nothing starts (exit `5`). Contracts are restored from the lock into the digest-keyed cache, and the Stage keeps pulled packages by digest in a cache that survives runs. (#577)
- **cli** ([Release v3.134.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.134.0)): `cratis run` hands the Stage the extension settings as `systems__<System>__<setting>` configuration, named secrets mapped to the settings that use them, registry credentials, the allow-list and the signing keys of the locked publishers. The Stage starts each backend in its tier when it is first needed. (#577)
- **cli** ([Release v3.134.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.134.0)): Documentation for extension settings, named secrets, registry credentials and allow-lists in the [environments](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/environments/index.md#extension-settings-and-secrets) and [run](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/reference/run.md#extensions) pages, linked with the Screenplay and Stage documentation. (#577)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.24.0)): `component <System>.<Component> <name>` on a screen places a component through the system that names the extension, not through a package. `property` lines are checked against the contract (`PLAY1114`, `PLAY1115`). (#1255)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.24.0)): `on <signal>` under an extension component handles a signal its contract `raises`, and `with <input> from signal.<value>` reads a value the signal carries (`PLAY1116`, `PLAY1117`). `on <System>.<Trigger>` anywhere on a screen handles an extension trigger, and `PLAY1112` reports one the contract does not offer.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.24.0)): `PLAY1118` warns when a `ui profile` targets a platform the component's contract does not list.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.24.0)): The UI contract (`export --format ui`, MCP `read-workspace` view `ui-contract`) carries each placement with the contract's `kind`, `platforms`, `placeholder` and the static placeholder HTML when the restored package holds a safe one, and lists the extensions each screen needs so a host can start their backends ahead of use.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.24.0)): Executable model ESM v52: a screen that places an extension component is admitted with `ExecutableScreens.Admit` as an ESM v52 screen with `extensions` and `components`; default compilations keep their version and bytes. The published `esm-v52.schema.json` and golden are included.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.24.0)): The event model board draws an extension component as an `extension-component` element named after the component.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.24.0)): The TypeScript compiler exports `checkExtensionUseSites` and reads `target platform` of a `ui profile`, matching the C# checks.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.24.0)): Monaco and VS Code highlight `signal.<value>` and document `component` and `signal`; the Invoicing sample places `Mailer.MailPreview` on `InvoiceBalance` and its vendored contract now lists `ios` and `android`.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.19.0)): Execution tiers for backends that come from extension packages: no process for frontend-only extensions, a shared host per runtime (`dotnet`, `node`, `jvm`) that isolates the extensions it loads, a native process, or a dedicated container. The deployment decides what may share a host with `Stage:Extensions:Execution:Sharing`; anything else needs a container configured under `Containers`, and a system without one is refused with a message that names the setting.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.19.0)): Lazy activation: a backend starts on the first ability call, proxied request or delivered inbound request that needs it, stops after its idle timeout (ten minutes by default) when nothing is in flight, and starts again for the next call. A call that finds its backend stopped waits for the start and is not lost.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.19.0)): Activation policy: the extension contract's default, overridden by the system's own `activation always | on demand [idle after <n> <unit>]` line in the model. `always` keeps the backend running and starts it again if it fails.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.19.0)): Frontend-aware activation: reaching a screen that places an extension component starts that extension's backend before the component's first call, with `POST /stage/extensions/activation/screens/{screen}`. Reaching the screen is the trigger and mounting a component is not; the reasoning is in the documentation. Which systems a screen places comes from `IScreenExtensionUsage`, which is empty until screens can use extension components ([Cratis/Screenplay#1255](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/issues/1255)).
- **Stage** ([Release v5.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.19.0)): Workbench visibility: `GET /stage/extensions/activation` and a script-free page at `/stage/extensions/activation/view` show each extension's tier, state (inactive, starting, active, stopping), policy, last activation and any reason it could not start.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.19.0)): Documentation: [Execution tiers and activation](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/execution-tiers.md), linked with the [extension packages](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/packages.md) page, the Screenplay [systems overview](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/systems.md) and [settings page](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/environments.md). Part of Cratis/Screenplay#1248. (#1066)
- **cli** ([Release v3.133.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.133.0)): `cratis extension verify [TARGET]` checks a package folder, or a package a registry serves (a catalog short name, an OCI reference or an npm package), and reports every check with a status and exact diagnostics. See https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/reference/extension-verify.md. (part of #576)
- **cli** ([Release v3.133.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.133.0)): Checks: the contract compiles with no errors or warnings; the manifest has a display name, a description, an icon and stated requirements, and the icon and marketplace images exist, are `png`, `jpg`, `webp` or `svg` by their content and are within the size limits; the package ships what the contract declares and nothing else (a folder lists what its build does not ship in `.extensionignore`); every placeholder is static HTML; the package digest and contract hash match `screenplay.lock`; and the contract is usable by the Screenplay compiler, with `--baseline <FOLDER>` to require a drop-in replacement for the previous release.
- **cli** ([Release v3.133.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.133.0)): A check that applies but could not run is reported as skipped, never as passed. The frontend (Scene conformance kit) and backend (protocol handshake) checks are skipped for now; `--strict` fails them, and warnings, too.
- **cli** ([Release v3.133.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.133.0)): Human-readable output by default and `--format json` on request. Exit code `0` when the package is correct, `5` when a check failed, `1` when the target or reference is not found, `2` when a registry is unreachable and `4` when its credentials are refused.
- **cli** ([Release v3.133.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.133.0)): A package that `cratis extension new` creates from templates that follow the documented template interface passes `cratis extension verify`.
- **Arc** ([Release v23.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.2.0)): Workload caller admission for Kubernetes workloads on capability ports, with tenantless execution and a token-review cache capped at 30 seconds (#3710)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.1.0)): `ISystemAbilities`, `SystemAbilities` and `IAbilityInvoker`: a system becomes an injectable, substitutable collaborator whose methods are its abilities, and each call is handed to the extension host with the command's causation (command, correlation, operation, phase) and an idempotency key that is stable for the call and differs between `Execute` and `Compensate`. A failed ability throws `AbilityInvocationFailed`, which fails the command and triggers compensation as for any other operation. (part of #3792)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.1.0)): The Screenplay generator reads an operation whose `Execute` and `Compensate` only call abilities into `invokes <System>.<Ability>` phases, declares each invoked system with its abilities, and supports operations that invoke more than one system. A phase that does anything else stays in code and reports why. (part of #3792)
- **Arc** ([Release v23.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v23.1.0)): Documentation: [Invoke Screenplay abilities from command operations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/commands/operations/abilities.md), linked with the Screenplay [operations](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/operations.md) and Stage [ability invocations](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/ability-invocations.md) pages. (part of #3792)
- **cli** ([Release v3.132.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.132.0)): `cratis extension new` creates a full extension (an OCI artifact) or a frontend-only component package from the `Cratis.Templates.Extensions` templates. A full extension takes a `--backend` (`csharp`, `kotlin`, `go`, `typescript` or `none`) and a `--frontend` (`react`, `mount`, `screenplay` or `none`); a frontend-only package takes a `--component` (`react` or `mount`). Every package gets a `contract.play` with its manifest, a placeholder icon and image, a static HTML placeholder per frontend component, a sample ability or component with specs, a README and the build and publish scripts. See https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/reference/extension-new.md. (part of #575)
- **cli** ([Release v3.132.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.132.0)): An interactive wizard that asks only what the flags did not answer. The wizard and the flags build the same request and are held to the same rules, so they create the same package. `--no-prompts` never asks; `--dry-run` lists the files; `--format json` reports them.
- **cli** ([Release v3.132.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.132.0)): `--template-path`, `--package` and `--version` choose where the templates come from. The CLI fails with exit code 2 and names the symbol when a template does not offer a choice the request needs.
- **cli** ([Release v3.132.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.132.0)): The template interface, the names of the templates and the symbols the CLI passes to them, is documented so the templates can be built against it.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.18.0)): Extension packages are loaded from the model's `screenplay.lock`: full extensions as OCI artifacts and frontend-only packages from npm. Stage fetches exactly the locked digest, never a tag or a version range, caches it by digest and hashes the bytes against the lock on every read. See https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/packages.md. (#1064)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.18.0)): Trust is decided by the deployment, never by the model: an allow-list of publishers and references in `.cratis/extensions.json`, narrowed by `Stage:Extensions:Packages:Allow`, and a signature that must verify against the key of the publisher the lock records (cosign key-based signatures for OCI, the registry signature for npm). Registry credentials come from `Stage:Extensions:Packages:Registries` only.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.18.0)): A package that is not allowed, not signed, signed by another key, has another digest or contract than the lock, needs another Stage or Screenplay version, ships a placeholder that runs script or is not safe to unpack is refused with its reason through `/stage/semantic/admission`. The model is not loaded while a package is refused, and a published host refuses to start.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.18.0)): Every ability of an extension is pinned to the locked package digest, so every workflow run pins the package that was admitted. `IExtensionPackages` and `LoadedExtensionPackage.UnpackBackend` expose the loaded packages and their verified backend bytes for activation.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.18.0)): Frontend assets are served at `/extension-assets/{id}/{path}`, and the manifest icon, images and each component's placeholder under `/stage/extensions/packages/{id}`. Placeholders are served with a content security policy that forbids scripts and external loads, and only into a frame.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.18.0)): `GET /stage/extensions` lists loaded and refused extensions with their manifest, images, contents and requirements, and `/stage/extensions/view` renders the same listing as a page.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.23.0)): `invokes <System>.<Ability>` in reactions, with the ability's inputs and one branch per answer: `on returned` (reads `$result.<field>`), `on failed` (reads `$failure.reason`) and `on <Outcome>` for a declared outcome. (#1252)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.23.0)): Workflow actions invoke abilities too, with an edge per outcome and values mapped from literals, the subject or an earlier node's result (`Node.field`). A `compute` node, an approval answered through an ability (`via`) and `expires after` parse, print and validate.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.23.0)): State entries invoke abilities: `entering invokes <System>.<Ability>`, or a bare `entering` with a C# body.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.23.0)): Specifications script an ability's answer with `given <System>.<Ability> returns`, `answers` or `fails` and assert the call with `then invokes`. The reference evaluator runs reactions that invoke abilities.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.23.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY1001` to `PLAY1017` cover unknown targets, unmapped or mistyped inputs, unreachable or duplicate branches, values read outside their branch and abilities that answer later.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.23.0)): Executable semantic model v51 carries reaction, workflow-action and state-entry invocations. Models that invoke no ability keep v50 or earlier.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.23.0)): The TypeScript compiler, the Monaco editor (completion, highlighting, hover), the VS Code grammar, the event model board and the MCP operation inventory (`invokedBy` call sites) understand the new forms.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.23.0)): A `Shipping` sample with a REST carrier and a chat system invoked from a reaction, a workflow and a state machine, and ability invocations in the `Invoicing` sample.
- **cli** ([Release v3.130.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.130.0)): `cratis extension restore` fills the extension cache from `screenplay.lock`. A reference the lock does not pin is resolved to the newest release in its range and pinned with its version, digest, contract hash and publisher. `--locked` fails on a missing or mismatched entry and never changes the lock. (part of #578)
- **cli** ([Release v3.130.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.130.0)): `cratis extension update [SYSTEM]` moves one system's extension, or every referenced extension, to the newest release in its range. It reports what each new contract adds, removes or changes, and every use in the model the new contract no longer offers, before the lock is written. `--dry-run` writes nothing; `--allow-incompatible` writes anyway.
- **cli** ([Release v3.130.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.130.0)): `cratis extension vendor <DESTINATION>` copies every locked package, with its signature, to a folder or an `oci://host/prefix` registry for air-gapped deployments. `cratis extension restore --source <DESTINATION>` then restores from the copy without reaching the original registries.
- **cli** ([Release v3.130.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.130.0)): Packages come from catalog short names (`"cratis/stripe"`), full OCI references and frontend-only package references. Private registries authenticate with credentials stored through `cratis config` under `extensions.registry.<host>.token` (or `username` and `password`); no command prints a credential.
- **cli** ([Release v3.130.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.130.0)): Trust: a package must be signed, its signature must be valid for its digest, and its publisher must be on the project's allow-list in `.cratis/extensions.json`. A first-time publisher is confirmed interactively and recorded; a non-interactive run fails instead. A publisher whose key changed is never offered for trust.
- **cli** ([Release v3.130.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.130.0)): Reference pages for the [extension commands](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/reference/extension.md), linked with the Screenplay [extension contracts and lock reference](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/extension-contracts.md).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.22.0)): The extension contract file: `display name`, `description`, `icon`, `image` with captions, `requires stage | screenplay | platform | runtime`, `setting <name> [public | secret]`, `ability`, `trigger`, `component` (`kind`, `platform`, `placeholder`, `property`, `raises`, `invokes`), `backend` and `frontend`, parsed by both the C# and the TypeScript compiler. A setting that is both public and secret is an error, so a secret can never reach a browser. See https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/extension-contracts.md. (#1253)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.22.0)): A system that names an extension gets the contract's abilities, and its triggers are available to reactions as `when <System>.<Trigger>`. Settings are checked against the contract.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.22.0)): `screenplay.lock`, a plain-text lock beside the model root that pins each reference's version, digest, contract hash and publisher, with a vendored `extensions` folder and a digest-keyed cache.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.22.0)): `screenplay restore [<folder>] [--locked] [--update] [--source <directory>] [--vendor]` to pin and restore extensions; `--locked` is for CI and never resolves a range.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.22.0)): Provider compatibility: a use of a trigger the extension no longer offers is reported at each use (`PLAY1112`), and `ExtensionCompatibility.Mismatches` compares two contracts.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.22.0)): Editors (`screenplay mcp`, the language service) restore unresolved references automatically and report them as warnings until they are restored.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.22.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY0998`, `PLAY0999` and `PLAY1100` to `PLAY1113`, Monaco and VS Code highlighting of contract lines, and a locked extension in `Samples/Invoicing`.
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.61.0)): A signed `urn:cratis:bearer:resource` claim on the forwarded bearer principal, set to the configured audience the token was validated against, so a backend can confirm the token was issued for the resource it serves (#324).
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.61.0)): A startup warning when a bearer route is configured but no ingress proxy is trusted, so every request would be refused (#323).
- **Stage** ([Release v5.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.17.0)): The built-in `http` extension. An ability's `http` block gives the method and path, headers, query, a structured body, what each response status returns, and which status settles with a named outcome. It decorates the registered ability backend, so abilities without a block still reach their connected extension backend. Register it with `AddStageHttpAbilities` after the invocations. It runs in-process; moving it to packaged extensions follows Cratis/Stage#1064. (#1069)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.17.0)): Base address and authentication (`none`, `bearer`, `basic` or `apiKey`) come only from the settings and secrets of the system, `systems:<System>:Http:*`. The model holds no credential: a block that sets `Authorization`, `Cookie`, `X-API-Key` or `Host` is an error. Secrets are never logged or put in a failure reason, and redirects are not followed.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.17.0)): A response with no matching line fails the ability: a client error other than a timeout rules the effect out, anything else (a server error, a timeout, a dropped connection) leaves it open for reconciliation. A missing setting or input is reported as not sent, naming the setting and never its value.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.17.0)): A reference page for the block: https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/http.md
- **Stage** ([Release v5.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.16.0)): Inbound requests with a method that may change something (`POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`) to `/extensions/<System>/...` are stored with their raw body and headers and acknowledged with `202 Accepted` before the extension is involved. An extension that is not running is activated to take delivery. (part of #1068)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.16.0)): Delivery with retries: `2xx` is delivered, a `4xx` such as `401` for a signature that does not verify is rejected for good and never retried, and `408`, `425`, `429`, `5xx` or no answer retry with a doubling wait until `MaxAttempts`, then show as failed. Every attempt carries the same delivery identity as `invocation_id` and `idempotency_key`.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.16.0)): Replay protection: a request identical in method, path, query and body to one already held is acknowledged and not delivered again, while the first is waiting and for `ReplayWindow` (24 hours). A request that was given up on is not treated as a replay, so a sender's retry is delivered.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.16.0)): A cap on waiting requests (`MaxOpen`, answers `503` with `Retry-After`) and a streamed body limit, so an anonymous sender cannot fill storage.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.16.0)): Workbench endpoints `GET /stage/extensions/deliveries` and `POST /stage/extensions/deliveries/retry` list inbound traffic with its status, attempts and last outcome, and retry a failed or rejected request. They never show a body or header values.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.16.0)): `correlation_key` on `RaiseTrigger`, so a raised trigger also resumes the workflow runs that wait for it about the same thing. `HostServices.Trigger` takes a `correlationKey`.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.16.0)): C# SDK `WebhookSignature` verifies an HMAC-SHA256 signature in constant time, with an optional signed timestamp and tolerance to refuse a captured request replayed later. `HttpCall` carries `DeliveryId` and `Attempt`.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.16.0)): Documentation: [Inbound traffic and webhooks](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/inbound-traffic.md), linked from the extensions overview, the protocol and host services references, the connect-a-backend guide and the C# SDK page.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.16.0)): Settings under `Stage:Extensions:Inbound`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.21.0)): `secret <Name>` (top level, optional `description`), `setting <name> from secret <Name>`, `setting <name> = <literal>`, `extension "<package>" version <v>` and `activation always | on demand [idle after <n> <unit>]` under a `system`, in both the C# and TypeScript compilers, with diagnostics `PLAY0990` to `PLAY0997`. (#1254)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.21.0)): Settings, secrets and activation are exposed on the semantic compilation as realization metadata; they change no executable-model bytes.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.21.0)): `EnvironmentRequirements` lists named secrets; unmapped settings read from `systems.<System>.<setting>`. `EnvironmentRequirements` stays C#-only.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.21.0)): Highlighting, completion and hover for the new lines in the Monaco language service and VS Code, plus documentation in Environments, Systems and Grammar and an Invoicing sample.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.15.0)): The extension protocol, `cratis.stage.extensions.v1`, as versioned `.proto` files under `Source/Extensions/Protocol`, linted with `buf`. Stage calls `Describe` (a handshake that checks the running backend against the contract of its system), `Invoke`, `HandleHttp` and `Health`; the backend calls `GetSetting`, `GetSecret`, `RaiseTrigger`, `Answer`, `Log` and `RecordMetrics`. Every request carries the tenant, invocation identity, idempotency key, attempt, deadline and trace context.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.15.0)): Durable ability invocations now reach a backend: set `Stage:Extensions:HostPort` and list each system's backend under `Stage:Extensions:Backends`. Without it nothing listens and abilities answer "not sent" as before. A backend that cannot be reached, fails the handshake or does not honor its contract is "not sent" and safe to retry; an error after the call was sent is "may have reached the system".
- **Stage** ([Release v5.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.15.0)): Host services on a separate gRPC port, scoped to one system by a session credential delivered only in the handshake. Secrets reach an extension only through `GetSecret`, never through its environment or a file. Raising a trigger needs a deduplication key and reports when nothing reacts to it. Logs and metrics are tied to the invocation's causation.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.15.0)): Inbound HTTP at `/extensions/<System>/<path>`, routed to the backend that declared the route. Cookies and authorization headers are not forwarded.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.15.0)): SDKs with their own tests, build and a minimal example: `Cratis.Stage.Extensions` (C#, `net10.0` and `net11.0`), `io.cratis.stage:stage-extensions` (Kotlin), a Go module and `@cratis/stage-extensions` (TypeScript). Each passes the shared conformance suite in `Source/Extensions/Conformance` over the wire.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.15.0)): Documentation: the [protocol reference](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/protocol.md), [host services reference](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/host-services.md), [connecting a backend](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/connect-a-backend.md), the [conformance suite](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/conformance.md) and a getting-started page for each SDK, linked with the Screenplay, Choreography and Scene documentation. The protocol and SDKs live in Cratis/Stage, and version one leaves out a key-value state store and an outbound credential proxy; the [overview](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/index.md) explains why. (#1065)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.14.0)): ESM v43 is audited. A `set once` mapping renders as Chronicle's `SetOnce`. A children collection renders its ordering: `insert first` as `Prepend()`, and `sort by` as `OrderBy` or `OrderByDescending` on the child property. The specification runner runs both, and the reference runner agrees on the set-once keep-first rule and on both sort directions. (part of #1056)
- **AI** ([Release v7.153.6](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.153.6)): A systems-and-abilities reference in the Screenplay automations skill: declaring a system and its abilities, choosing `safe`, `idempotent` or `once`, typed inputs, `returns`, outcomes and `answers later`. (part of #1121)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.13.0)): Durable ability invocations: an invocation is recorded with the fact, run or transition that caused it before anything runs, then performed by a Choreography run under the capability `ability:<System>.<Ability>@<contract major>` with the extension package digest pinned. Its identity is derived from the origin's semantic address and causation, never the attempt, and is the grain key, the run's deduplication key and the idempotency key the extension receives. (#1067)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.13.0)): Effect classes: `safe` retries freely, `idempotent` retries with the same key, and `once` is never retried when an attempt may have reached the system. It stops for reconciliation instead, including when a restart interrupts an attempt.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.13.0)): `GET /stage/invocations` lists invocations and the ones stopped for reconciliation. `POST /stage/invocations/{identity}/reconciliation` records which outcome happened and the origin continues from that choice. `POST /stage/invocations/{identity}/answer` answers an ability that answers later, after checking the identity against the persona or policy the invocation names. Both are sandbox tooling and are not mapped in a published runtime.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.13.0)): Per-system gates from deployment configuration under `Stage:Invocations:Systems`: `MaxConcurrent`, `RequestsPerSecond` and `MaxAttempts`.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.13.0)): Documentation: [Durable ability invocations](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/ability-invocations.md), linked with the Screenplay reactions, workflows, state machines and operations pages and with the Choreography runtime pages.
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.6.0)): `useCanvasPresence` in `@cratis/choreography/collaboration/react` shares this session's position on a canvas: it holds the room's connection while the canvas is mounted, announces the canvas as soon as it opens, maps the pointer into content space, follows the content under a pointer that stands still while the canvas pans or zooms, and leaves the area when the canvas closes. Wire its `handlePointerMove` onto the canvas and call `handleTransformChange` when it pans or zooms. See [Send the pointer position](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/blob/main/Documentation/collaboration/frontend.md#send-the-pointer-position).
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.6.0)): `contentPointOf` and `reportCanvasPresence` in `@cratis/choreography/collaboration` do the same steps without React, and `PresenceSink` describes what they send to.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.20.0)): `ability <Name> [safe | idempotent | once]` under a `system`, with typed inputs, a `returns` block, `outcome <Name>` lines, `answers later`, and an `AbilityEffect` implementation (`file`, tagged fence or `implementation` wrapper). An omitted effect class means `once`, which is never retried. Both compilers parse, validate, print and round-trip them, held equal by a new conformance document (#1250).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.20.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY0983` (duplicate ability, compared ignoring case), `PLAY0984` (unknown effect class), `PLAY0985` (outcome named `next`, `refused`, `failed`, `returned` or `expired`) and `PLAY0986` (undeclared input or returned type). Malformed abilities, outcomes with a payload and empty `returns` blocks report `PLAY0495`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.20.0)): Executable semantic model v50: a model that declares an ability carries `application.systems` with each ability's effect class, typed inputs and returns, outcomes, `answersLater` and realization (`none` or `code`). Models without abilities keep their bytes and version. The ESM v50 JSON Schema and golden vector are published.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.20.0)): A "Systems and abilities" guide, grammar and diagnostics reference entries, and an ability-declaring `PaymentGateway` system in the Invoicing sample.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.20.0)): Ability completion, hover, validation and highlighting in the Monaco language service and the VS Code grammar, and ability details in the MCP `system-intent-details` view.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.156.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.156.0)): The production image ships `createdump`, so setting `DOTNET_DbgEnableMiniDump=1` writes a crash dump. The production and Kubernetes hosting pages describe how to enable, size, collect and safely handle dumps (#5814)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.156.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.156.0)): `connectedClients.subscriptionCheckIntervalSeconds` (default 60) and `connectedClients.subscriptionSettleSeconds` (default 30) control how often reactor and reducer subscriptions are checked once they are known to be intact, and for how long they are checked closely after a silo leaves the cluster (#5816)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.156.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.156.0)): The container resources page publishes measured container profiles for 1, 2 and 4 CPUs with the default, Workstation and classic Server garbage collectors, and the benchmark harness accepts `--cpus` and `--memory` to measure your own sizes (#5110)
- **Scene** ([Release v5.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v5.3.0)): New npm package `@cratis/scene.extensions` 1.0.0: the extension component contract with two component kinds, `reactComponent(Component)` (web only) and `mountComponent((host, properties, context) => { update, unmount })`, a framework-neutral floor for Vue, Svelte, Lit, web components or plain DOM. (#296)
- **Scene** ([Release v5.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v5.3.0)): `ExtensionContext` with the user, tenant, locale, theme, public settings, strings, `invoke`, `raise`, `on` and change notifications, and `createExtensionContext` for hosts, previews and specs to build and change one.
- **Scene** ([Release v5.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v5.3.0)): React hooks `useExtensionContext`, `useUser`, `useTenant`, `useLocale`, `useTheme`, `useSettings`, `useStrings`, `useInvoke`, `useRaise` and `useTrigger`, which re-render only when their own part changes, with `ExtensionContextProvider`.
- **Scene** ([Release v5.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v5.3.0)): Composition in both directions: `MountedComponent` lets a React component host a mount component, `context.renderSlot` and `ExtensionSlot` host Scene content, and `mountExtensionComponent` gives a host one path for both kinds.
- **Scene** ([Release v5.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v5.3.0)): Frontend manifest types and `validateFrontendManifest` for the `component` part of an extension contract.
- **Scene** ([Release v5.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v5.3.0)): A conformance kit, `createExtensionConformanceChecks`, that runs the same expectations against a component of either kind.
- **Scene** ([Release v5.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v5.3.0)): `resolveExtensionComponent` in `@cratis/scene.engine` resolves extension components from a UI profile's packages alongside runtime packages, including shadowing and platform support.
- **Scene** ([Release v5.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v5.3.0)): Documentation: a [contract reference](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/contract.md) and a [how-to for building a component in React and with mount](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/extensions/build-a-component.md), linked with the [Screenplay screens page](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/screens.md) and [Stage custom packages](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/custom-packages.md).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.19.0)): A workflow `action <Node> invokes <Command>` has a `refused` outlet, separate from `failed`. A refused command is not retried, and an unconnected `refused` outlet fails the run. (part of #1251)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.19.0)): A deprecation migration note in the state machine and workflow documentation, and a specification section for state machines and workflows.
- **cli** ([Release v3.128.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.128.0)): `cratis render --upgrade-profile` moves an existing Cratis application to the newest scaffold profile (.NET 11) (#579)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.12.0)): The generated policy reads the caller's identifier from the principal Arc authorized: the `urn:cratis:identity:subject` claim first, then the name identifier claim, then `sub`. A principal that carries none, or two different values of the first claim it has, makes the policy unavailable, so it denies, including for `!=`. An application that identifies callers another way implements the partial method `ResolveCallerId` on the generated `PolicyValues` class. (#1061)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.12.0)): A specification's caller can name its identifier with `id "alice"`; the generated test puts it in the principal's name identifier claim, and the in-process specification runner evaluates the policy the same way.
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.5.0)): `WorkflowNodeOutcome.Suspended(key, expiresAfter)`: a performer that accepted the work answers later. The node waits like a `wait` without holding a worker, and ends when it is completed by key or expires through the `expired` outlet (#200)
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.5.0)): `IWorkflowCompletions.Complete` completes the suspended node by `WorkflowCompletionKey`, and `WaitKind.Completion` is a new wait kind
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.5.0)): `WorkflowsOptions.CapabilityLimits` bounds the performer calls of a capability in flight on a silo, by capability id or a prefix such as `ability:Slack.*`, through `IWorkflowCapabilityGates`
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.5.0)): `IWorkflowRun.Reconcile` resumes a run stopped on an ambiguous outcome with a chosen success, failure or skip, recorded as `WorkflowNodeReconciled` with the actor. `IWorkflowRun` is now at `[Version(1)]`
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.5.0)): `NodeExecutionSnapshot.Reason` and `NodeExecutionSnapshot.IdempotencyKey` tell a person what was ambiguous and which invocation to look up
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.5.0)): Documentation: suspension by key, capability gates and reconciliation, linked with the Stage invocation work
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.23.0)): The `:observation_reconnect` client option (`base_delay`, `max_delay`, `healthy_stream_duration`, in milliseconds) tunes the stream reconnect backoff, and invalid values are rejected when the client starts
- **Stage** ([Release v5.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.11.0)): `CratisRendering.CreateProfile` has an overload that takes a scaffold profile version. Version 3 (net10.0) stays the default; passing `"4"` creates a net11.0 profile that the artifact render planner admits, and an unsupported version is refused with an error naming the supported versions (#1078)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.10.0)): The Host and the spec runner can pass a scaffold profile version to the body worker (`Stage:BodyWorker:ProfileVersion` for the Host, `--body-worker-profile-version` for the spec runner). Version 4 analyzes method bodies against .NET 11 references. When no version is set, version 3 (.NET 10) is used as before. An unsupported version is rejected at startup with a message that names the supported versions. (#1074)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.9.0)): Version 4 of the Cratis render profile renders applications that target .NET 11; version 3 keeps targeting .NET 10 (part of #1047)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.185.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.185.1)): `CratisProxiesSkip` (alias `DisableProxyGenerator`) MSBuild property that skips TypeScript proxy generation for a build, for example `-p:CratisProxiesSkip=true` (#3799).
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.155.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.155.0)): Client packages now target `net11.0` alongside `net10.0` (#5763)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.154.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.154.1)): A Kubernetes hosting guide with a reference manifest tested on a three-node cluster: StatefulSet with anti-affinity and a PodDisruptionBudget, startup, readiness and liveness probes on a dedicated exclusive health port, a hardened security context for the chiseled image, NetworkPolicies for the kernel's ports, guidance for running with TLS and authentication off, choosing between `Recreate` and rolling updates, and handling the connection string, certificates and the encryption certificate (#5793)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.154.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.154.1)): The container resources page describes a small-container profile and a throughput profile, with the garbage collector settings for each and the commands to measure them on your own workload (part of #5110)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.154.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.154.0)): Observer state and job progress are published as metrics: failed and quarantined partitions and running state per observer (reported only while an observer needs attention, then as zero for a short while after it recovers), resident observers per event store, namespace and type, running catch-up and replay jobs, catch-up and replay progress per observer, and `cratis.chronicle.event_sequence.append.duration` as a histogram in seconds. The maximum number of series per metric can be set with `Telemetry:MetricSeriesLimit` (#4610)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.154.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.154.0)): Opt-in standby for start-before-stop rollouts (`Clustering:SingleWriterLease:Standby`). A replacement kernel waiting for the single-writer lease reports ready with a `Standby` status so Azure Container Apps, Cloud Run and Kubernetes rolling updates can stop the old instance. Event processing starts only once the lease is held, client calls during standby fail with a retryable status, and `Clustering:SingleWriterLease:StandbyStartupTimeout` bounds how long a kernel that holds the lease may keep reporting standby while it starts (#5238)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.154.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.154.0)): `observers:maxConcurrentSubscriptions` bounds how many observer subscriptions and failed-partition recoveries a silo runs at once across all event stores. By default it is sized to the machine's CPUs
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.154.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.154.0)): `observers:idleDeactivationAfter` releases an observer that has no subscription and no pending work after it has been idle for that many seconds (900 by default, `0` turns it off). Releasing an idle observer frees about 40 KB of memory, and a benchmark in `Benchmarks/Chronicle.Benchmarks.Kernel` measures what an activation retains (#5792)
- **AI** ([Release v7.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.153.0)): Host-prepared workspace mode for Docker workers: set `CRATIS_WORKSPACE_MODE=host-prepared` and `CRATIS_WORKSPACE_HOST_PATH` to mount an existing checkout at `/workspace` instead of cloning. The path is validated before launch, git metadata is mounted read-only, and the worker leaves its results as uncommitted working-tree changes. Kubernetes workers refuse host-prepared jobs with a clear error (#1070)
- **AI** ([Release v7.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.153.0)): `CRATIS_GIT_DEADLINE_SECONDS` (default 600) bounds every git command a worker runs; a command that exceeds it is stopped and reported as `[git-timeout]` (#1069)
- **Choreography** ([Release v2.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Choreography/releases/tag/v2.4.0)): `net11.0` targets alongside .NET 10, using the .NET 11 `System.Text.Json` and `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging.Abstractions` packages on .NET 11 (#197)
- **Dockerfiles** ([Release v8.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Dockerfiles/releases/tag/v8.1.0)): .NET 11 base development images (`cratis/base:dotnet11` and `cratis/base-mongodb:dotnet11`) built on the .NET 11 GA ASP.NET Core runtime for Ubuntu 26.04; the existing .NET 10 images and tags are unchanged (#26)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.8.0)): An action on a screen is checked against the server before it is offered. The server decides from the same policy that guards the command, so a policy that reads recorded state, such as the plan of the asset on the screen, answers for that asset. A refused action is shown disabled with the reason on hover, and is asked again when the data the screen reads changes. Only an explicit refusal withholds the action: an unanswered or failed check leaves it offered, and the server still decides when the command runs. (part of #1050)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.17.0)): `SemanticScreenAvailability.Evaluate` reports every element of a screen as available, unavailable or undecided for a caller. It names the policies that gate the element and why it is withheld. A policy that decides from recorded state is evaluated against the data the screen is showing, so one asset's plan can offer an action that another's withholds. A missing instance denies, and an opaque predicate is undecided rather than available. The report presents the fact and never replaces enforcement. (part of #335)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.7.0)): Audit of ESM v48 (agent exposure) and v49 (the caller's identifier in a policy state condition). Agent exposure is a contract for the hosting surface, so the generated backend neither renders nor refuses it.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.7.0)): A policy that compares a recorded property with `$identity.id`, and a caller fixture that names an identifier, are refused with STAGE-ESM-016. Stage has no mapping from Arc's authorization principal to that identifier yet, and a wrong guess would let the wrong caller through. (part of #1061)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.16.0)): `article.author == $identity.id` (or `!=`) in a policy state condition, over a required text property. A caller with no identifier gives nothing to compare, so the condition is false and a policy that cannot tell who is asking never allows. (#1233)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.16.0)): `id "alice"` on a `given caller` fixture, the value `$identity.id` reads when a specification runs.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.16.0)): ESM v49, selected only by a model that uses either; every earlier model keeps its bytes and revision. The conformance corpus and the C# and TypeScript compilers cover it.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.153.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.153.1)): The production hosting page recommends an exclusive health port (`Cratis__Chronicle__Health__Exclusive=true`) together with the dedicated health port, explains that the Workbench must then be served from the main port, and the Workbench hosting page shows the reverse proxy setup that works with it, including how to trust the kernel's certificate (#5794)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.153.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.153.1)): The container resources page has a measured sizing example from a production kernel, with the queries to reproduce each figure, a rule of thumb for memory per grain activation, guidance that CPU is sized for cold start, and a guide to choosing between a shared kernel and a kernel per tenant (#5795)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.15.0)): An `agents` block declares an explicit, default-deny list of the queries and commands an AI agent may call, each with a required description and a `read` or `write` scope, for personal access tokens. The executable semantic model carries it as ESM v48, and the compiler, MCP server, CLI, Monaco editor and VS Code extension all understand it. (part of #1165)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v6.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v6.15.0)): `screenplay export --format capability-manifest` and the MCP `capability-manifest` workspace view emit the `screenplay-capability-manifest/1` allowlist from the `agents` block only. Nothing is emitted when the model has errors or no `agents` block, and a failed export removes an earlier manifest file. The schema and a paired example are published at https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/capability-manifest-v1.schema.json. (#1199)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.6.0)): Rendering of `narrow <Policy>` on a collection query (ESM v47) as an Arc row narrowing. The generated narrowing decides each row with the policy's reads keyed by the row's own properties, and Arc applies the result before sorting, paging and counting. A row whose state is absent, unreadable or rejected is dropped. (#1049)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.14.0)): `Sequence.Import` imports entries in bulk from an `iter.Seq2[Entry, error]`: every event is validated before anything is committed, entries are read as they upload and sent in chunks, and the commit runs on the kernel as a job.
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.14.0)): `Sequence.BeginImport` opens an explicit `ImportSession` for adding entries in steps, and `Sequence.ReattachImport` resumes a session by its `ImportID` after a dropped connection.
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.14.0)): `Entry` carries an imported event's correlation id, caused-by identity, generation, source sequence number and idempotency key. Appends, batches and units of work refuse entries that set them, rather than dropping them silently.
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.14.0)): The `contracts/imports` package with the generated Imports service contracts.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.22.0)): `Chronicle.Imports.import/2` imports any `Enumerable`, a lazy `Stream` included: every event is validated before anything is committed, the source is read while it uploads and sent in chunks, and the commit runs on the kernel as a job. `import_async/2` runs it as a `Task`.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.22.0)): Explicit import sessions: `begin/1` opens one, `Chronicle.Imports.Session.add/2` adds events in steps, `complete/1` and `abandon/1` end it, and `reattach/2` resumes a session by its import id after a dropped connection.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.22.0)): `get/2` reads an import's state, and `observe/2` delivers its progress as messages to a process until `unobserve/1`.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.22.0)): `EventForEventSourceId` carries an event's correlation id, generation, source sequence number, idempotency key and named tags, which an import keeps on the stored events.
- **Stage** ([Release v5.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.5.0)): Rendering of Screenplay policies with `reads` and state conditions (ESM v44 to v47): the generated policy reads each declared read model by the key the operation supplies, and a missing key, a missing instance or a failing read denies. A negated condition cannot turn an absent instance into an allow. Text and membership conditions (`contains`, `starts with`) and typed policy inputs with `authorize` mappings render the same way. (#1048)
- **Stage** ([Release v5.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v5.5.0)): The ESM pairs v44 to v47 are audited. A query that narrows its rows (v47) is refused with STAGE-ESM-016 until list narrowing renders, so no model silently returns every row. (part of #1049)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.62.0)): `eventLog.import(events, options?)` imports events in bulk from an `AsyncIterable` or an `Iterable`: every event is validated before anything is committed, the events are read as they upload and sent in chunks, and the commit runs on the kernel as a job.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.62.0)): `beginImport(options?)` opens an explicit import session for producers that are not a single stream, with `add`, `complete`, `abandon` and a `progress()` stream, and `beginImport(importId, options?)` reattaches to a session after a dropped connection, continuing after the chunks the kernel already acknowledged.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.62.0)): `EventForEventSourceId` gains `correlationId`, `causedBy`, `generation` and `sourceSequenceNumber`, which an import keeps on the stored events.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.62.0)): `ReactorScenario` delivers the events of one `events(...)` call, or of a `page(...)` across event sources, to reactor batch methods as one page; `shouldHaveFailed` and `shouldHaveDeferred` assert what a batch method settled.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): `eventLog.import(events)` imports events in bulk from a `Flow` or a `Sequence`: every event is validated before anything is committed, the events are read lazily and sent in chunks, the commit runs on the kernel as a job, and progress is reported while it runs. Imported events reach every observer once.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): `beginImport()` opens an explicit import session for adding events in steps, and `beginImport(importId)` reattaches to a session after a dropped connection.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): Java callers get the same through `importEvents(...)` and `beginImport(...)` on the blocking event sequence, returning `CompletableFuture`.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): `EventForEventSourceId` carries an event's correlation id, caused-by identity, generation, source sequence number and idempotency key, which an import keeps on the stored events.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): `EventBatch` exposes the partitions a batch method deferred and failed.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): `ReadModelScenario` runs reducers whose method takes an `EventBatch`, calling it once per fold the way Chronicle delivers a page; a failed partition rethrows its error and a deferred one throws `ReducerDeferred`.
- **AI** ([Release v7.151.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.151.0)): Agent worker callbacks report why a push of committed work failed as a fixed category (permission, non-fast-forward, hook, authentication, network or unknown), without including git's error output. (#1100)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.153.0)): Client reducers that take pages receive events in pages spanning many event sources on live delivery, catch-up and replay. The kernel reads the current read models of a page together, writes the event sources that succeeded together, and writes nothing for an event source the reducer failed or deferred. See [Batches for reducers](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/reducers/batches.mdx).
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.153.0)): `[BatchSize]` on a reactor or reducer declares the most events and bytes in one page and, for reducers, how many event sources of a page are reduced at once (`MaxConcurrency`, the processor count by default). `MaxDelayMilliseconds` is accepted but not applied yet.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.153.0)): The kernel honors the page bounds a client declares on every path, capped by the new `Observers.MaxEventsPerPage` (1 000 by default) and `Observers.MaxBytesPerSubscriberCall` (4 MiB by default) settings. A delivery above the bounds is sent as several pages in order, and a single event larger than the byte bound is sent on its own. See [Observers configuration](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/hosting/configuration/observers.md).
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.153.0)): Analyzers CHR0070 to CHR0077 report batch method mistakes at compile time: `[Observes<T>]` on a method that does not take an `EventBatch`, a batch method that observes nothing, an event type that reaches more than one method, a `BatchContext` on a per-event method, a reactor batch method that returns something, a reducer that mixes batch and per-event methods, a batch signature Chronicle cannot call, and a warning for a pattern on an event type the method does not observe. See [Code analysis](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/code-analysis/index.md).
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.153.0)): Registering a reactor or reducer refuses a batch method whose parameters Chronicle cannot supply, and a per-event reducer method that takes a `BatchContext`. A batch method may declare `CancellationToken cancellationToken = default`.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.153.0)): `ReactorScenario` runs batch methods: the events of one `Events(...)` call, or of `Given.Page(...)` across event sources, reach them as one page, and `ShouldHaveFailed` and `ShouldHaveDeferred` assert what a batch method settled. `ReadModelScenario` runs batch reducers and surfaces a deferral as `ReducerDeferred`.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.153.0)): Event sequences can import large volumes of events through a resumable import session: `eventLog.Import(events)` takes an `IAsyncEnumerable` or `IEnumerable` and streams it in chunks, and `BeginImport` gives an explicit session that can be reattached by `ImportId`. Every event is validated before anything is committed, the commit runs as a job that survives restarts without appending twice, ordinary appends keep flowing during the commi
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.59.0)): Opt-in DPoP proof verification on OAuth bearer routes: `DPoP` authorization scheme, ES256 proofs only, `htm`, `htu` (from the configured public origin only), `iat` window, `ath` access-token binding and `cnf.jkt` key binding, with `WWW-Authenticate` challenges (part of #258).
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.59.0)): A shared Redis replay store so a proof `jti` cannot be reused on another replica, with fail-closed handling when the store is unavailable, its clock is off or it was just provisioned (part of #258).
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.59.0)): Optional server-issued DPoP nonces, bound to the key and origin (part of #258).
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.59.0)): DPoP configuration documentation covering the public origin, the replay store and deployment requirements (part of #258).
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.13.0)): Graph projections: the `graphs` package declares nodes with `graphs.NewNode[T]()` (one field tagged `chronicle:"key"`), edges with `graphs.NewEdge[T](source, target)` and the graph with `graphs.New(name, elements...).Build()`, registered with `Registry.AddGraph`. See [Graph projections](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/blob/main/Documentation/projections/graphs.md).
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.13.0)): Graph node and edge options `From`, `RemovedWith` (with `graphs.Detach()` for nodes), `UsingKey`, `UsingSourceKey`, `UsingTargetKey`, `UsingIdentity`, `OnlyWhenExisting`, mappings `Set`, `SetFromContext`, `SetFromEventSourceID`, `SetValue`, `Add`, `Subtract`, `Increment`, `Decrement`, `Count`, `Clear`, and declaration errors as `*graphs.DeclarationError` matchable with `errors.Is`.
